q-0001simple3I have an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass and I just moved to a new apartment last week. How soon do I need to update MOM with my new address, and how do I do it?You need to update MOM **within 2 weeks** of changing your residential address [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/notify-mom/index.md]. Since you moved last week, you still have time, but should do this promptly to avoid penalties. **How to update:** 1. Ensure your new address meets the relevant [housing requirements](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/work-permit-for-foreign-worker/housing/various-types-of-housing) 2. Log in to [EP eService](https://www.mom.gov.sg/eservices/services/employment-pass-eservice) using your Singpass to update your address—the update is immediate 3. Print the acknowledgement page and keep it with your pass card [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/notify-mom/index.md]The answer correctly states the 2-week deadline, the penalty warning, and all three steps (housing requirements, EP eService login, print acknowledgement). It matches the reference answer substantively with helpful additional context.
Question:
I have an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass and I just moved to a new apartment last week. How soon do I need to update MOM with my new address, and how do I do it?
Expected:
You need to update MOM within 2 weeks of changing your residential address. Otherwise, you may be penalised. To update: (1) Ensure that your new address meets the relevant housing requirements. (2) Log in to EP eService to update your address. The update is immediate. (3) Print the acknowledgement page and keep it with your pass card.
Actual:
You need to update MOM **within 2 weeks** of changing your residential address [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/notify-mom/index.md]. Since you moved last week, you still have time, but should do this promptly to avoid penalties.

**How to update:**

1. Ensure your new address meets the relevant [housing requirements](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/work-permit-for-foreign-worker/housing/various-types-of-housing)
2. Log in to [EP eService](https://www.mom.gov.sg/eservices/services/employment-pass-eservice) using your Singpass to update your address—the update is immediate
3. Print the acknowledgement page and keep it with your pass card [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/notify-mom/index.md]

q-0002simple3We have an EP holder on an EP (Sponsorship) whose pass is expiring soon. I want to make sure I submit the renewal in time — how early can I start the renewal, and how long should I expect processing to take? Also, once we get the IPA letter, how long is it valid for?For EP (Sponsorship) renewal, here's the timeline you need to know: **When you can start renewing:** You can begin applying to renew **3 months before the pass expires** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md]. **Processing time:** It takes around **8 weeks to process the renewal application** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/ep_sponsor_renewal_form.md]. For most cases, you can expect a decision **within 6 weeks** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md]. **IPA letter validity:** Once your renewal is successful, you'll receive an in-principle approval (IPA) letter that is valid for **3 months** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md]. You must get the pass issued before the IPA or current pass expires, whichever comes first, to avoid an overstaying fine.The chatbot accurately covers all key points: 3-month early renewal window, processing time of 6-8 weeks, 3-month IPA validity, and the requirement to issue before IPA or current pass expires to avoid overstaying fine. The answer is substantively equivalent to the reference.
Question:
We have an EP holder on an EP (Sponsorship) whose pass is expiring soon. I want to make sure I submit the renewal in time — how early can I start the renewal, and how long should I expect processing to take? Also, once we get the IPA letter, how long is it valid for?
Expected:
For EP (Sponsorship), you can start applying to renew 3 months before the pass expires (as opposed to the standard EP, which allows renewal up to 6 months / 180 days before expiry). You must apply before the expiry date. Processing time for EP (Sponsorship) renewals is within 6 weeks for most cases (the renewal form itself states around 8 weeks). Once the renewal is approved, you will receive an in-principle approval (IPA) letter that is valid for 3 months. You must get the pass issued before the IPA or the current pass expires, whichever occurs first — otherwise an overstaying fine will be imposed. The duration of the renewed pass will start when the current pass expires. Any remaining validity from the current pass (up to six months) is added on to the renewal, so renewing early does not reduce or lose validity. The renewed pass can be granted for up to 3 years.
Actual:
For EP (Sponsorship) renewal, here's the timeline you need to know:

**When you can start renewing:**
You can begin applying to renew **3 months before the pass expires** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md].

**Processing time:**
It takes around **8 weeks to process the renewal application** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/ep_sponsor_renewal_form.md]. For most cases, you can expect a decision **within 6 weeks** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md].

**IPA letter validity:**
Once your renewal is successful, you'll receive an in-principle approval (IPA) letter that is valid for **3 months** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md]. You must get the pass issued before the IPA or current pass expires, whichever comes first, to avoid an overstaying fine.
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot accurately covers all key points: 3-month early renewal window, processing time of 6-8 weeks, 3-month IPA validity, and the requirement to issue before IPA or current pass expires to avoid overstaying fine. The answer is substantively equivalent to the reference.

q-0003simple3I'm looking into the Employment Pass for a candidate we want to hire in Singapore. What's the minimum salary needed to qualify? The minimum salary needed to qualify for an Employment Pass depends on the candidate's age and sector [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]: **For most sectors (except financial services):** - Current requirement: $5,600 minimum (increasing progressively with age to $10,700 at age 45+) - From 1 January 2027: $6,000 minimum (increasing to $11,500 at age 45+) **For the financial services sector:** - Current requirement: $6,200 minimum (increasing to $11,800 at age 45+) - From 1 January 2027: $6,600 minimum (increasing to $12,700 at age 45+) This qualifying salary is the first stage of eligibility. Even if a candidate meets this salary requirement, they must also pass the second stage—the points-based COMPASS assessment—to be approved for the Employment Pass [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md].The chatbot's answer is accurate and comprehensive, covering the minimum salary ($5,600), progressive increase with age (up to $10,700 at 45+), and higher requirements for financial services sector. It also provides additional useful information about future requirements and the COMPASS assessment.
Question:
I'm looking into the Employment Pass for a candidate we want to hire in Singapore. What's the minimum salary needed to qualify?
Expected:
The qualifying salary for an Employment Pass is a fixed monthly salary comparable to the top one-third of local PMET salaries. It starts from $5,600 and increases progressively with age, up to $10,700 for candidates in their mid-40s. Candidates in the financial services sector need higher salaries than these amounts to qualify. The actual value varies with age and sector of employment.
Actual:
The minimum salary needed to qualify for an Employment Pass depends on the candidate's age and sector [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]:

**For most sectors (except financial services):**
- Current requirement: $5,600 minimum (increasing progressively with age to $10,700 at age 45+)
- From 1 January 2027: $6,000 minimum (increasing to $11,500 at age 45+)

**For the financial services sector:**
- Current requirement: $6,200 minimum (increasing to $11,800 at age 45+)
- From 1 January 2027: $6,600 minimum (increasing to $12,700 at age 45+)

This qualifying salary is the first stage of eligibility. Even if a candidate meets this salary requirement, they must also pass the second stage—the points-based COMPASS assessment—to be approved for the Employment Pass [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md].
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot's answer is accurate and comprehensive, covering the minimum salary ($5,600), progressive increase with age (up to $10,700 at 45+), and higher requirements for financial services sector. It also provides additional useful information about future requirements and the COMPASS assessment.

q-0004simple3We have a candidate who's been approved for an ONE Pass. His common-law partner wants to come along and eventually work here. What type of pass does the common-law spouse get, and can they work in Singapore on that pass?Based on the knowledge base, here's what applies to your candidate's common-law partner: **Pass type:** The common-law spouse can obtain a [Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP)](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/index.md) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/index.md]. **Working in Singapore:** Yes, common-law spouses holding an LTVP can work in Singapore by applying for a [Letter of Consent (LOC)](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md). The application is free and typically takes up to 4 weeks to process [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md]. **Timing note:** The knowledge base recommends applying for family passes once the ONE Pass is approved, since LTVP applications take 4 weeks to process [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/index.md].The chatbot correctly identifies LTVP as the pass type for common-law spouses of ONE Pass holders and confirms they can work via Letter of Consent (LOC), matching the reference answer with additional helpful details about processing time and timing recommendations.
Question:
We have a candidate who's been approved for an ONE Pass. His common-law partner wants to come along and eventually work here. What type of pass does the common-law spouse get, and can they work in Singapore on that pass?
Expected:
A common-law spouse (defined as a common-law relationship between 2 people that is recognised under the laws of their country or region) of an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder is eligible for a Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP). Common-law spouses on LTVP can apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC) to work in Singapore.
Actual:
Based on the knowledge base, here's what applies to your candidate's common-law partner:

**Pass type:** The common-law spouse can obtain a [Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP)](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/index.md) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/index.md].

**Working in Singapore:** Yes, common-law spouses holding an LTVP can work in Singapore by applying for a [Letter of Consent (LOC)](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md). The application is free and typically takes up to 4 weeks to process [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md].

**Timing note:** The knowledge base recommends applying for family passes once the ONE Pass is approved, since LTVP applications take 4 weeks to process [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/index.md].
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot correctly identifies LTVP as the pass type for common-law spouses of ONE Pass holders and confirms they can work via Letter of Consent (LOC), matching the reference answer with additional helpful details about processing time and timing recommendations.

q-0005simple2I'm an employer planning to apply for an Employment Pass for a candidate. How long does it take to process the application, and how much is the application fee?Based on the Employment Pass application process [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/apply-for-a-pass/index.md]: **Processing time:** - If you apply online: Processed or given an update within **10 business days** - For overseas companies without a Singapore-registered company: within **6 weeks** for most cases **Application fee:** - **$105**, payable by GIRO, Visa, Mastercard, or Amex Note that you can check your application status online after 10 business days (or 6 weeks for overseas companies). If additional documents are required, your processing time may extend beyond these timeframes.Covers key points (10 business days for online, 6 weeks for overseas, $105 fee, longer if additional docs needed) but omits that overseas companies pay via PayNow instead of GIRO, and doesn't mention the local sponsor requirement for sponsorship cases.
Question:
I'm an employer planning to apply for an Employment Pass for a candidate. How long does it take to process the application, and how much is the application fee?
Expected:
The processing time and fee depend on whether the employer has a Singapore-registered company or not. For employers with a Singapore-registered company applying online via the EP eService, the application is processed or given an update within 10 business days, and the application fee is $105, payable by GIRO, Visa, Mastercard, or Amex. For overseas companies without a Singapore-registered company (sponsorship cases), a local sponsor must submit the application, the processing time is within 6 weeks for most cases (some cases may take longer), and the application fee is also $105, payable by PayNow, Visa, Mastercard, or Amex. In either case, if additional information or documents are required, the processing time may take longer than the stated timeframes.
Actual:
Based on the Employment Pass application process [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/apply-for-a-pass/index.md]:

**Processing time:**
- If you apply online: Processed or given an update within **10 business days**
- For overseas companies without a Singapore-registered company: within **6 weeks** for most cases

**Application fee:**
- **$105**, payable by GIRO, Visa, Mastercard, or Amex

Note that you can check your application status online after 10 business days (or 6 weeks for overseas companies). If additional documents are required, your processing time may extend beyond these timeframes.
Judge remark (scored 2):
Covers key points (10 business days for online, 6 weeks for overseas, $105 fee, longer if additional docs needed) but omits that overseas companies pay via PayNow instead of GIRO, and doesn't mention the local sponsor requirement for sponsorship cases.

q-0006simple3We have a candidate who wants to apply for the ONE Pass, but our company is not publicly listed. Can we use our company valuation to qualify as an established company, or is there another way for non-listed companies to meet the criteria?Based on the knowledge base, non-listed companies cannot use company valuation to meet the "established company" criteria for the ONE Pass (Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass). [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md] For a company to be considered established, it must have **at least one of the following**: - Market capitalisation of at least US$500 million, or - Annual revenue of at least US$200 million [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md] However, there is an alternative path: if your company does not meet these criteria, your candidate can **re-apply for the ONE Pass after earning a fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 for 12 consecutive months** with your company. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/faq/one-pass/does-my-prospective-employer-need-to-meet-the-criteria-for-an-established-company.md] Alternatively, your candidate may be eligible for other work passes such as the Employment Pass, Personalised Employment Pass, EntrePass, or Tech.Pass. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/faq/one-pass/does-my-prospective-employer-need-to-meet-the-criteria-for-an-established-company.md]The chatbot correctly states that company valuation cannot be used, explains that non-listed companies must meet the US$200 million annual revenue criterion, provides the alternative path of earning S$30,000 for 12 consecutive months, and mentions other work pass options—all matching the reference answer.
Question:
We have a candidate who wants to apply for the ONE Pass, but our company is not publicly listed. Can we use our company valuation to qualify as an established company, or is there another way for non-listed companies to meet the criteria?
Expected:
No, company valuation is not accepted as an alternative to market capitalisation. Non-publicly listed companies must meet the annual revenue criterion of at least US$200 million to be considered established companies, as they do not have market capitalisation. If the prospective employer does not meet the established company criteria, the candidate may still apply for the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass after earning a fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 for the 12 consecutive months leading up to the date of application. Alternatively, the candidate may apply for another work pass such as the Employment Pass, Personalised Employment Pass, EntrePass, or Tech.Pass.
Actual:
Based on the knowledge base, non-listed companies cannot use company valuation to meet the "established company" criteria for the ONE Pass (Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass). [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md]

For a company to be considered established, it must have **at least one of the following**:
- Market capitalisation of at least US$500 million, or
- Annual revenue of at least US$200 million

[source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md]

However, there is an alternative path: if your company does not meet these criteria, your candidate can **re-apply for the ONE Pass after earning a fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 for 12 consecutive months** with your company. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/faq/one-pass/does-my-prospective-employer-need-to-meet-the-criteria-for-an-established-company.md]

Alternatively, your candidate may be eligible for other work passes such as the Employment Pass, Personalised Employment Pass, EntrePass, or Tech.Pass. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/faq/one-pass/does-my-prospective-employer-need-to-meet-the-criteria-for-an-established-company.md]

q-0007simple3We're applying for an Employment Pass for a candidate whose qualification verification through a background screening company has been pending for over two weeks. The candidate's university is one of the institutions listed in the application form's drop-down list. Do we have to wait for the verification to come back before we can submit the EP application, or is there some workaround?You don't have to wait for the verification to come back. Since your candidate's institution is listed in the application form's drop-down list of accredited institutions, you can submit a **receipt for the verification service in lieu of the verification proof** if verification is still pending after 14 working days [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/bsc-guide.md § Page 2]. Here's what happens next: once your EP application is approved, you can bring in the candidate and get their pass issued without needing to submit the full verification proof. Question:
We're applying for an Employment Pass for a candidate whose qualification verification through a background screening company has been pending for over two weeks. The candidate's university is one of the institutions listed in the application form's drop-down list. Do we have to wait for the verification to come back before we can submit the EP application, or is there some workaround?
Expected:
If your verification is still pending after 14 working days and your awarding institution is from the application form's drop-down list of accredited institutions, you can submit the receipt for the verification service in lieu of the verification proof in the work pass application. Once the work pass application is approved, you can bring in the candidate and get their pass issued. You do not need to submit the verification proof to MOM after that. However, if the qualification is found to be false, the work pass will be revoked and the candidate must leave Singapore.

Question:
We have a candidate who earned S$30,000/month overseas for the past 12 months at a company with annual revenue of US$180 million and market cap of US$450 million. The company's global offices combined have annual revenue exceeding US$200 million though. Does the company qualify as established, and what documents do we need to submit for this candidate?
Expected:
For a company to be considered established, it must have at least one of the following: market capitalisation of at least US$500 million, or annual revenue of at least US$200 million. Combined amounts from the entire global office can also be considered and will be assessed on a case-by-case basis. So while the individual office does not meet either threshold on its own, the combined global revenue exceeding US$200 million may qualify, but this will be assessed case-by-case by MOM.

For a candidate whose current or last held employment was overseas, the following documents are required:
1. Passport page (including any amendments). If the candidate's name in their passport differs from their name in other documents, an explanation letter and supporting documents such as a deed poll are also needed.
2. Monthly payslips for the 12 consecutive months leading up to the date of application.
3. Employment verification proof from a background screening company.
4. Documents supporting other income sources other than fixed monthly salary (if applicable).
5. Latest financial information of the candidate's employer obtained within the last 3 months, including documents indicating the latest market capitalisation or annual company revenue. If this information is not available, the company's latest audited Profit & Loss statements may be provided instead. MOM will only consider documents from third party auditors; documents signed by the company's management staff will not be accepted.

Additionally, if the candidate drew salaries from more than one employer in the 12 consecutive months leading up to the date of application, the qualifying fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 (or its equivalent in foreign currency) should come from one employer only. Other sources of income may be considered on a case-by-case basis.
Actual:
Based on the information provided, **the company does not qualify as established** under the standard criteria. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md]

For a company to be considered established, it must have at least one of the following:
- Market capitalisation of at least US$500 million, or
- Annual revenue of at least US$200 million

Your candidate's employer has a market cap of US$450 million (below the US$500 million threshold) and annual revenue of US$180 million (below the US$200 million threshold). While the combined global offices exceed US$200 million in annual revenue, the knowledge base states this "will be assessed on a case-by-case basis," but does not confirm it automatically qualifies. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md]

**For documents**, since the candidate earned S$30,000/month overseas for 12 consecutive months, you would need to submit: [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/documents-required.md]

- Monthly payslips for the 12 consecutive months leading up to application
- Employment verification proof from a background screening company
- Latest financial information of the employer (within last 3 months), including market capitalisation or annual revenue documents
- Documents supporting any other income sources (if applicable)
- Passport page

I recommend contacting MOM directly to clarify whether the combined global revenue of US$200+ million would qualify this company as established.

q-0009simple3We want to appoint an EP holder from another company to our Board of Directors. How long does the approval process take, what documents do we need, and how long is the approval valid for?To appoint an Employment Pass holder from another company to your Board of Directors, here's what you need to know [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/taking-up-secondary-directorship/index.md]: **Processing time:** Within 5 weeks. **Documents required:** The Employment Pass holder's appointment letter to take up directorship. **Validity:** The approval is valid up to the expiry or cancellation date of the current EP. Note that you'll need to apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC) from MOM, and the approval is generally granted only if your company is related by shareholding to the EP holder's employer (as reflected in ACRA records) and the secondary directorship is related to their primary employment role.The chatbot accurately provided all key information: 5 weeks processing time, appointment letter as required document, and validity tied to EP expiry/cancellation. It also correctly mentioned the LOC requirement and added relevant context about eligibility conditions.
Question:
We want to appoint an EP holder from another company to our Board of Directors. How long does the approval process take, what documents do we need, and how long is the approval valid for?
Expected:
The process takes within 5 weeks. The document required is the Employment Pass holder's appointment letter to take up directorship. The validity of the Letter of Consent (LOC) is up to the expiry or cancellation date of the EP holder's current Employment Pass. When the EP is successfully renewed, you need to apply for a new LOC.

Question:
We're looking to hire a carbon credit trader and want to claim the COMPASS C5 Skills Bonus. What are the eligibility requirements for the candidate under the SOL for that role?
Expected:
For the carbon credit trader/broker role on the SOL, the candidate must minimally meet both criteria (1) and (2): (1) Minimum total three years' work experience in carbon markets or carbon credit-related fields. (2) At least a degree-equivalent qualification (i.e. score 10 points on COMPASS C2. Qualifications criteria). Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG) will assess all applications that meet these criteria and may contact employers for a business profile, including details on their carbon trading business. The supporting agency is EnterpriseSG. Note that the job duties for this role include trading or brokering carbon credits, originating and structuring carbon credit deals, facilitating transactions between buyers and sellers of carbon credits, and performing carbon market research and analysis on topics such as different types of carbon credits and their attributes, quality of carbon credits (e.g. Core Carbon Principles and Assessment Framework), factors that affect price dynamics in over-the-counter markets or exchanges, and international regulations and policies regarding carbon markets (e.g. Paris Agreement Article 6) as well as global and national compliance schemes and their evolving regulations (e.g. Australia, China, EU ETS). Additionally, the candidate needs to perform the job duties listed for the specific shortage occupation, meet the additional requirements as stated in the SOL Employer Guide, and you need to select the shortage occupation in the EP application. The candidate should select one of the eligible job titles in the EP application form, which must be the same as the occupation for the job advertisement in MyCareersFuture (unless exempted from the FCF job advertising requirement). This SOL was released in November 2025 and applies to new and renewal EP applications from 1 January 2026.

q-0011simple3My company is lowering the salary of one of our EP holders. How far in advance do we need to notify MOM, and what happens if the new salary doesn't meet the qualifying threshold?Based on the knowledge base, here's what you need to do: **Notification Timeline:** You must submit a request to notify MOM **at least 1 month** before the date you intend to lower the salary. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/notify-mom-of-changes/index.md] **If the New Salary Doesn't Meet the Qualifying Threshold:** The EP holder must continue to qualify for the Employment Pass with the revised salary. If the new salary falls below the [EP qualifying salary](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility#ep-qualifying-salary) requirement, you have two options: 1. Use the Self-Assessment Tool (SAT) to check if they would still meet the qualifying salary requirement 2. If they no longer qualify, you should cancel the work pass and apply for a new work pass based on their eligibility under the new salary [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/notify-mom-of-changes/index.md]The chatbot's answer covers all essential points: 1-month advance notification, requirement to continue qualifying, using SAT to check eligibility, and canceling/reapplying if they don't qualify. The information is accurate and comprehensive.
Question:
My company is lowering the salary of one of our EP holders. How far in advance do we need to notify MOM, and what happens if the new salary doesn't meet the qualifying threshold?
Expected:
As an employer, you must submit a request to MOM at least 1 month before the date you intend to lower the salary. The pass holder must continue to qualify for the work pass they are holding with the revised salary. You may use the Self-Assessment Tool (SAT) to find out if they are likely to meet the qualifying salary. If they do not qualify with the revised salary, you should cancel the work pass and apply for a new work pass based on the pass holder's eligibility.
Actual:
Based on the knowledge base, here's what you need to do:

**Notification Timeline:**
You must submit a request to notify MOM **at least 1 month** before the date you intend to lower the salary. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/notify-mom-of-changes/index.md]

**If the New Salary Doesn't Meet the Qualifying Threshold:**
The EP holder must continue to qualify for the Employment Pass with the revised salary. If the new salary falls below the [EP qualifying salary](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility#ep-qualifying-salary) requirement, you have two options:

1. Use the Self-Assessment Tool (SAT) to check if they would still meet the qualifying salary requirement
2. If they no longer qualify, you should cancel the work pass and apply for a new work pass based on their eligibility under the new salary

[source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/notify-mom-of-changes/index.md]

Incorporation in Singapore 2. Minimum investment of USD $10 million over the past 36 months **received from EDBI, SEEDS Capital, or other recognised investment firms** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c6-strategic-economic-priorities-sep-bonus-eligible-programmes/annex-c---eligible-programmes-for-sep-bonus.md] While you meet the incorporation and investment amount requirements, the funding must come from EDBI, SEEDS Capital, or other recognised investment firms. Question:
We're a high-growth startup incorporated in Singapore and we want to know if we can qualify for the SEP bonus under COMPASS. We've raised about USD 12 million over the last 3 years from a VC that isn't EDBI or SEEDS Capital. Does that count, or does the funding have to come from specific investors?
Expected:
High-growth startups incorporated in Singapore can qualify for the SEP bonus if they have a minimum investment amount of USD 10 million over the past 36 months. The funding must have been received from EDBI, SEEDS Capital, or other recognised investment firms (there is a separate list of recognised investment firms maintained for this purpose). So your startup would meet the investment threshold (USD 12 million exceeds the USD 10 million minimum) and the 36-month timeframe, but the VC firm you received funding from must be on the list of recognised investment firms. If it is not EDBI or SEEDS Capital, it needs to be one of the other firms on that recognised list. This programme is supported by Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG), and you can contact them at ENTERPRISESG_COMPASS@enterprisesg.gov.sg for queries, including whether your investor qualifies.
Actual:
Based on the COMPASS criteria, your startup would **not** qualify for the SEP bonus under the current eligible programmes. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c6-strategic-economic-priorities-sep-bonus-eligible-programmes/annex-c---eligible-programmes-for-sep-bonus.md]

The high-growth startup programme requires two conditions to be met:
1. Incorporation in Singapore
2. Minimum investment of USD $10 million over the past 36 months **received from EDBI, SEEDS Capital, or other recognised investment firms** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c6-strategic-economic-priorities-sep-bonus-eligible-programmes/annex-c---eligible-programmes-for-sep-bonus.md]

While you meet the incorporation and investment amount requirements, the funding must come from EDBI, SEEDS Capital, or other recognised investment firms. A VC that isn't one of these specified sources would not qualify.

However, you may explore other eligible SEP programmes that your startup might participate in, or contact Enterprise Singapore directly at ENTERPRISESG_COMPASS@enterprisesg.gov.sg to inquire whether your VC investor is considered a "recognised investment firm" under the framework. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c6-strategic-economic-priorities-sep-bonus-eligible-programmes/index.md]

q-0013simple3We're hiring a 30-year-old candidate for a role in our banking company. What fixed monthly salary does he need to score points under the COMPASS C1 salary criterion, and is there a way to get the maximum 20 points?For a 30-year-old candidate in the Banking & Other Financial Services Activities sector, here are the C1 salary thresholds: **Salary Requirements for C1 Points:** - **10 points**: Fixed monthly salary of **$11,251** or above (65th percentile) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/c1-salary-benchmarks.md] - **20 points (maximum)**: Fixed monthly salary of **$17,401** or above (90th percentile) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/c1-salary-benchmarks.md] Yes, there is a way to get the maximum 20 points—your candidate simply needs to meet or exceed the $17,401 monthly salary threshold for their age group.The chatbot correctly identified the salary thresholds ($11,251 for 10 points, $17,401 for 20 points) for a 30-year-old in the Banking sector, explained how to get maximum points, and noted the EP qualifying salary requirement. The clarifying question about sector was appropriate given the context-dependent nature of the benchmarks.
Question:
We're hiring a 30-year-old candidate for a role in our banking company. What fixed monthly salary does he need to score points under the COMPASS C1 salary criterion, and is there a way to get the maximum 20 points?
Expected:
For the Banking & Other Financial Services Activities n.e.c. sector, a 30-year-old candidate needs a fixed monthly salary of at least $11,251 to score 10 points under C1 (this is the 65th percentile of local PMET salaries in that sector). To score the maximum 20 points, the candidate needs at least $17,401 (the 90th percentile). These benchmarks were released in August 2025 and are updated once a year to reflect the latest market conditions. The required salary increases progressively with age, from age 23 to age 45. For this sector, the range starts at $7,510 (10 points) / $11,151 (20 points) for candidates aged 23 or below, and rises to $19,266 (10 points) / $30,793 (20 points) for candidates aged 45 or above. Additionally, candidates who do not meet the EP qualifying salary will not be eligible for an EP regardless of COMPASS points scored under C1. EP candidates earning at least $22,500 in fixed monthly salary are exempted from COMPASS entirely.
Actual:

- **10 points**: Fixed monthly salary of **$11,251** or above (65th percentile) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/c1-salary-benchmarks.md]
- **20 points (maximum)**: Fixed monthly salary of **$17,401** or above (90th percentile) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/c1-salary-benchmarks.md]

Yes, there is a way to get the maximum 20 points—your candidate simply needs to meet or exceed the $17,401 monthly salary threshold for their age group.

q-0014simple1I have an EP holder working for us who does manual labour. Do I need to buy medical insurance or work injury compensation insurance for them? I'm confused about what's actually required for EP holders versus other pass types.For an Employment Pass (EP) holder who does manual labour, here's what's actually required: **Medical Insurance:** Not mandatory. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/are-employers-required-to-buy-medical-insurance-for-employment-pass-holders.md] Providing medical insurance is optional—you can choose to include it in the employment contract or encourage your EP holder to obtain their own coverage. **Work Injury Compensation (WIC) Insurance:** Only mandatory if your EP holder earns less than $1,600 per month. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/does-an-employer-need-to-buy-wic-insurance-for-an-ep-holder.md] However, regardless of whether you've purchased insurance, you remain obligated to pay compensation for eligible workplace injury claims. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/what-are-my-medical-obligations-as-the-employer-of-an-ep-holder.md] **Other obligations:** You must provide paid sick leave (including dental leave) under the Employment Act. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/what-are-my-medical-obligations-as-the-employer-of-an-ep-holder.md] The key difference is that medical and WIC insurance requirements are stricter for Work Permit and S Pass holders, but EP holders have more flexibility.The chatbot incorrectly states WIC insurance is only mandatory for EP holders earning less than $1,600/month, omitting that it's also mandatory for ALL manual workers regardless of salary. Since the user specifically mentioned their EP holder does manual labour, this is a critical omission that could lead to non-compliance.
Question:
I have an EP holder working for us who does manual labour. Do I need to buy medical insurance or work injury compensation insurance for them? I'm confused about what's actually required for EP holders versus other pass types.
Expected:
Providing medical insurance is not a requirement for employing Employment Pass (EP) holders. Most EP holders may already have their own medical insurance or may want to choose their own coverage. For EP holders without existing medical insurance, employers can choose to provide insurance coverage as part of the employment contract, or encourage them to get their own. The medical insurance requirement applies only to Work Permit and S Pass holders, in line with employers' existing responsibility for the upkeep and maintenance of their migrant workers, including the provision of medical treatment.

However, for work injury compensation (WIC) insurance, buying it is only mandatory if you are employing manual workers or non-manual workers earning less than $1,600 a month. So if your EP holder is a manual worker, you are required to purchase work injury compensation insurance for them. Whether or not you have purchased WIC insurance, you are still required to pay compensation for an eligible claim.

Additionally, under the Work Injury Compensation Act (WICA), you are obligated to pay any eligible claims for workplace injuries. You are also obligated to provide paid sick leave, including dental leave, to any employee covered under the Employment Act.
Actual:
For an Employment Pass (EP) holder who does manual labour, here's what's actually required:

**Medical Insurance:** Not mandatory. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/are-employers-required-to-buy-medical-insurance-for-employment-pass-holders.md] Providing medical insurance is optional—you can choose to include it in the employment contract or encourage your EP holder to obtain their own coverage.

**Work Injury Compensation (WIC) Insurance:** Only mandatory if your EP holder earns less than $1,600 per month. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/does-an-employer-need-to-buy-wic-insurance-for-an-ep-holder.md] However, regardless of whether you've purchased insurance, you remain obligated to pay compensation for eligible workplace injury claims. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/what-are-my-medical-obligations-as-the-employer-of-an-ep-holder.md]

**Other obligations:** You must provide paid sick leave (including dental leave) under the Employment Act. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/faq/employment-pass/what-are-my-medical-obligations-as-the-employer-of-an-ep-holder.md]

The key difference is that medical and WIC insurance requirements are stricter for Work Permit and S Pass holders, but EP holders have more flexibility.

Question:
We have a candidate who's currently on a Dependant's Pass under an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder — not the spouse, just a dependent. They want to work for us. Can they just get a Letter of Consent like spouses do, or do they need a separate work pass?
Expected:
Only spouses of Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holders can work or operate a business in Singapore with a Letter of Consent (LOC). Other dependants who are not spouses will need to obtain a work pass to work for an employer in Singapore. If the dependant obtains an EP or S Pass, they will be granted the work and stay privileges associated with that pass, their stay will no longer depend on the main Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder, and they will need to cancel their Dependant's Pass before the EP or S Pass is issued. If the dependant obtains a Work Permit instead, they must hold on to both the Work Permit and the Dependant's Pass; the Work Permit validity will be tied to the DP's validity; they can apply for any Work Permit regardless of nationality or citizenship; they are not subject to the six-monthly medical examination, security bond, or pregnancy restrictions; and they do not need medical insurance from the employer if they are already covered under a medical insurance plan that meets MOM's prevailing minimum mandatory insurance coverage requirement.

Question:
I'm an EP holder earning $6,000 a month. I'd like to bring my parents over to Singapore — is that possible?
Expected:
No, not at a salary of $6,000 a month. While EP holders earning at least $6,000 a month can bring in certain family members (legally married spouse and unmarried children under 21 on a Dependant's Pass; common-law spouse, unmarried handicapped children aged 21 and above, and unmarried step-children under 21 on a Long-Term Visit Pass), parents can only be brought in on a Long-Term Visit Pass if the EP holder earns at least $12,000 a month.

Question:
We're looking to hire a carbon credit trader and want to claim the COMPASS C5 Skills Bonus under the SOL. What are the requirements the candidate needs to meet for that specific role?
Expected:
For the Carbon credit trader role on the SOL, the candidate must minimally meet both of the following criteria: (1) Minimum total three years' work experience in carbon markets or carbon credit-related fields, and (2) At least a degree-equivalent qualification (i.e. score 10 points on COMPASS C2. Qualifications criteria). Additionally, Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG) will assess all applications that meet these criteria and may contact employers for a business profile, including details on their carbon trading business. The supporting agency is EnterpriseSG. Note that unlike many other SOL occupations where MOM verifies the firm's involvement in the relevant space directly with agencies, for this role it is EnterpriseSG that assesses the applications. The candidate also needs to perform the listed job duties, which include: trading or brokering carbon credits; originating and structuring carbon credit deals; facilitating transactions between buyers and sellers of carbon credits; and performing carbon market research and analysis on topics such as different types of carbon credits and their attributes, quality of carbon credits (e.g. Core Carbon Principles and Assessment Framework), factors affecting price dynamics in OTC markets or exchanges, and international regulations and policies regarding carbon markets (e.g. Paris Agreement Article 6) as well as global and national compliance schemes and their evolving regulations (e.g. Australia, China, EU ETS). You must also select the shortage occupation in the EP application, and the job title selected must match the occupation used for the job advertisement on MyCareersFuture (unless exempted from the FCF job advertising requirement). This SOL was released in November 2025 and applies to new and renewal EP applications from 1 January 2026.

q-0018simple2My partner holds an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass, and I'm their common-law spouse on a Long-Term Visit Pass issued by MOM. I want to work in Singapore — what do I need to do, and how long will it take to get approved?To work in Singapore as a common-law spouse on a Long-Term Visit Pass, you need to apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC). Here's what you need to do: **Application Process:** 1. Your prospective employer must download and complete the [Letter of Consent (Common-Law Spouse) application form](https://www.mom.gov.sg/-/media/mom/documents/services-forms/passes/loc_cls_application_form.pdf) 2. Your employer submits the form with relevant supporting documents online [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md] **Timeline:** The application typically takes **within 4 weeks for most cases** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore.md]. The LOC is valid up to the expiry date of your Long-Term Visit Pass, so ensure your pass has at least 3 months validity remaining before applying [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore.md].Correctly identifies LOC requirement and employer-submitted application process, but states processing time as 4 weeks instead of 8 weeks per reference, and omits key details like supporting documents required, submission URL, and regulated occupation requirements.
Question:
My partner holds an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass, and I'm their common-law spouse on a Long-Term Visit Pass issued by MOM. I want to work in Singapore — what do I need to do, and how long will it take to get approved?
Expected:
As a common-law spouse holding a Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP) issued by MOM under an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder, you need to apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC). Your prospective employer must complete the application on your behalf by: (1) downloading and filling in the Letter of Consent (Common-Law Spouse) application form, and (2) submitting the completed form with the relevant supporting documents online at go.gov.sg/mom-submit-loc-cls. The form should be completed using Adobe Acrobat Reader DC, as it may not appear correctly with other PDF readers. The form can only be used if the LTVP was issued by the Ministry of Manpower. If the LTVP is valid for less than 3 months, it must be renewed before submitting the application. The duration applied for can be up to 60 months, though if approved, the period granted may be shorter than what was indicated. It takes around 8 weeks to process the application. Supporting documents required include: the original completed and signed application form (1 PDF, up to 7 MB), the travel document page showing the applicant's personal particulars (1 PDF or JPG, up to 1 MB), and — if applicable — an SFA licence for food establishments (1 PDF, up to 2 MB), a support letter from Enterprise Singapore for Representative Office applications (1 PDF, up to 3 MB), and registration or support letters from the relevant vetting agency/professional body/accreditation agency if the applicant will take on regulated occupations such as Dentist (Singapore Dental Council), Doctor (Singapore Medical Council), Emergency Medical Technician or Paramedic (Unit for Prehospital Emergency Care), Lawyer (Legal Services Regulatory Authority), Diagnostic radiographer/Occupational therapist/Physiotherapist/Radiation therapist/Speech therapist (Allied Health Professionals Council), Nurse (Singapore Nursing Board), Pharmacist (Singapore Pharmacy Council), TCM practitioner (Traditional Chinese Medicine Practitioners Board), or Football player/Coach (Sport Singapore) (1 PDF, up to 3 MB). Non-English documents must be accompanied by an English translation done by a translation service provider. MOM may also ask for additional documents during review. Note: Long-Term Visit Pass holders who are not common-law spouses cannot use this LOC process and must instead obtain a work pass (Employment Pass, S Pass, or Work Permit) through a prospective employer, meeting all the criteria for the relevant pass.
Actual:
To work in Singapore as a common-law spouse on a Long-Term Visit Pass, you need to apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC). Here's what you need to do:

**Application Process:**
1. Your prospective employer must download and complete the [Letter of Consent (Common-Law Spouse) application form](https://www.mom.gov.sg/-/media/mom/documents/services-forms/passes/loc_cls_application_form.pdf)
2. Your employer submits the form with relevant supporting documents online [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md]

**Timeline:**
The application typically takes **within 4 weeks for most cases** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore.md]. From 1 Jan 2027 for new applications, and for renewal of passes expiring from 1 Jan 2028, the qualifying salary will increase to $9,650. These figures are part of a progressive scale that increases with age from age 23, starting at $6,200 (current) / $6,600 (new) for age 23 or below, up to $11,800 (current) / $12,700 (new) at age 45 and above for the financial services sector.

q-0020medium3We're bringing in an integrated circuit engineer for our semiconductor fab. The candidate has a Bachelor's in Physics. I want to make sure we meet the requirements for this role under COMPASS — does the candidate need to satisfy both the semiconductor firm verification and the degree requirement, or is one sufficient? And do we need to submit any proof to MOM that we're in the semiconductor space?For an integrated circuit engineer role in the semiconductor space under COMPASS, your candidate **must meet both criteria** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md]: 1. **Be from a firm involved in the Semiconductor space** — MOM verifies this directly with EDB, so **no additional documentation is required** from you. 2. **At least a Bachelor's Degree in an engineering/science faculty** — Your candidate's Bachelor's in Physics qualifies, as Physics falls within the science faculty requirement. Since MOM handles the semiconductor firm verification directly with EDB, you don't need to submit separate proof of your firm's semiconductor involvement. However, if your firm is new to the semiconductor space, you should reach out to EDB to ensure proper registration [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md].The chatbot's answer is accurate and comprehensive, covering both criteria requirement, MOM-EDB verification process, no documentation needed, Physics degree qualification, and the EDB outreach option for new entrants.
Question:
We're bringing in an integrated circuit engineer for our semiconductor fab. The candidate has a Bachelor's in Physics. I want to make sure we meet the requirements for this role under COMPASS — does the candidate need to satisfy both the semiconductor firm verification and the degree requirement, or is one sufficient? And do we need to submit any proof to MOM that we're in the semiconductor space?
Expected:
The candidate must meet both criteria simultaneously — it is not sufficient to satisfy only one. First, the candidate's employer must be a firm involved in the Semiconductor space; MOM verifies this directly with EDB, so no additional documentation from the employer is required. However, new entrants to the Semiconductor space can reach out to EDB. Second, the candidate must hold at least a Bachelor's Degree in an engineering or science faculty, which includes but is not limited to qualifications relating to those fields. A Bachelor's in Physics would fall under a science faculty and should satisfy the degree requirement. Since MOM verifies the firm's semiconductor involvement directly with EDB, the employer does not need to submit additional documentation for that criterion.
Actual:
For an integrated circuit engineer role in the semiconductor space under COMPASS, your candidate **must meet both criteria** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md]:

1. **Be from a firm involved in the Semiconductor space** — MOM verifies this directly with EDB, so **no additional documentation is required** from you.

2. **At least a Bachelor's Degree in an engineering/science faculty** — Your candidate's Bachelor's in Physics qualifies, as Physics falls within the science faculty requirement.

Since MOM handles the semiconductor firm verification directly with EDB, you don't need to submit separate proof of your firm's semiconductor involvement. However, if your firm is new to the semiconductor space, you should reach out to EDB to ensure proper registration [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md].

Would he still qualify for the skills bonus under the shortage occupation list?Yes, your candidate can qualify for the C5. Skills bonus under the Shortage Occupation List (SOL) for a cybersecurity engineer role. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/upcoming-changes-to-employment-pass-eligibility/complementarity-assessment-framework-compass/compass-shortage-occupation-list.md] For cybersecurity roles on the SOL, candidates need to meet **either** of these requirements: - **(1a)** At least a Bachelor's degree in Computer Science, Information Technology, Programming & Systems Analysis, or Science (Computer Studies), **OR** - **(1b)** Minimum total three years' work experience in one of these job titles: Cybersecurity architect, Cybersecurity engineer, ICT security engineer, ICT security architect, or Information systems security developer Since your candidate has 4 years of work experience as an ICT security architect, he meets requirement (1b) and qualifies for the SOL bonus points, even without a degree. Yes, he would still qualify. Since your candidate has 4 years of experience as an ICT security architect — which is one of the listed job titles and exceeds the 3-year minimum — he would meet the requirement under pathway (1b) even without a Bachelor's degree.

I want to understand the COMPASS salary benchmarks — what fixed monthly salary do we need to hit for the C1 criterion to score points, and what's the difference between the 10-point and 20-point thresholds at that age?# COMPASS C1 Salary Benchmarks To score points under the C1 criterion, your candidate's fixed monthly salary needs to meet or exceed the **65th percentile of local PMET salaries in your sector** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/index.md]. The specific salary thresholds vary by sector and are updated annually. **Point thresholds:** - **20 points**: 90th percentile and above - **10 points**: 65th to less than 90th percentile - **0 points**: Below 65th percentile [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md] To find the exact salary figures for your candidate's age and your organisation's sector, you'll need to download the **C1 salary benchmarks by sector** document (most recently released in August 2025) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/index.md]. Note that the benchmarks increase progressively with age from age 23 to 45, so the thresholds for a 30-year-old will be specific to that age bracket. **Important:** Even if your candidate scores points under C1, they must still meet the separate EP qualifying salary requirement, or the application will likely be rejected [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md].The chatbot correctly explains the percentile-based point thresholds but fails to provide the specific salary figures ($6,987 for 10 points, $10,620 for 20 points) and the difference ($3,633) that the user explicitly asked for and that the reference answer provides.
Question:
We're putting together an EP application for a candidate who's 30 years old. I want to understand the COMPASS salary benchmarks — what fixed monthly salary do we need to hit for the C1 criterion to score points, and what's the difference between the 10-point and 20-point thresholds at that age?
Expected:
For a candidate aged 30, the required salary for 10 COMPASS points (which corresponds to the 65th percentile of local PMETs) is $6,987, and the required salary for 20 points (the 90th percentile of local PMETs) is $10,620. The difference between the two thresholds at age 30 is $3,633.
Actual:
# COMPASS C1 Salary Benchmarks

To score points under the C1 criterion, your candidate's fixed monthly salary needs to meet or exceed the **65th percentile of local PMET salaries in your sector** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/index.md]. The specific salary thresholds vary by sector and are updated annually.

**Point thresholds:**
- **20 points**: 90th percentile and above
- **10 points**: 65th to less than 90th percentile  
- **0 points**: Below 65th percentile

[source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]

To find the exact salary figures for your candidate's age and your organisation's sector, you'll need to download the **C1 salary benchmarks by sector** document (most recently released in August 2025) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/index.md]. Note that the benchmarks increase progressively with age from age 23 to 45, so the thresholds for a 30-year-old will be specific to that age bracket.

**Important:** Even if your candidate scores points under C1, they must still meet the separate EP qualifying salary requirement, or the application will likely be rejected [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md].

This programme falls under Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG).

Question:
We're hiring someone who currently holds a Dependant's Pass tied to an ONE Pass holder. She'll be coming on board on a Work Permit. Her current medical insurance already meets MOM's minimum mandatory coverage requirement. Do we still need to provide her with medical insurance, and are there any other sector-specific requirements she'd be exempt from?

If the DP holder already has medical insurance that meets MOM's prevailing minimum mandatory insurance coverage requirement, the employer does not need to provide medical insurance. In addition, a DP holder (tied to an ONE Pass) working on a Work Permit is exempt from the six-monthly medical examination, the security bond requirement, and pregnancy restrictions. There are also no nationality or citizenship restrictions on the Work Permit application, and the Work Permit validity will be tied to the DP's validity.
Actual:

**Medical Insurance:**
No, you do not need to provide her with medical insurance. Since she already holds medical insurance that meets MOM's prevailing minimum mandatory insurance coverage requirement, she is exempt from the employer medical insurance requirement. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore.md]

**Other Sector-Specific Exemptions:**
As a Dependant's Pass holder obtaining a Work Permit, she is exempt from:
- Six-monthly medical examination
- Security bond
- Pregnancy restrictions

[source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore.md]

q-0029complex2We're looking to hire a foreign specialist for a role that's on the Shortage Occupation List. Our candidate graduated from KU Leuven in Belgium. When we submit the EP application, do we just pick any job title, or is there a specific process we need to follow for the SOL role — and will the candidate's university help with the application?For an SOL role, you cannot simply pick any job title—there is a specific process you must follow: **Job Title Selection:** You must select one of the **eligible job titles** listed for the specific shortage occupation in your EP application form. This job title must match the occupation advertised on MyCareersFuture, unless you are exempted from the job advertising requirement. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md] **Additional Requirements:** Your candidate must meet the additional requirements specific to that shortage occupation, which can include: - Relevant qualifications (uploaded in the qualifications section) - Work experience in relevant job titles (selected in the work experience section) - Registration proof (uploaded in the agency support question) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md] The candidate must also perform the actual job duties listed for that specific shortage occupation. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md] **Regarding the university:** The knowledge base does not contain information about whether the candidate's university (KU Leuven) plays a role in the application process. You should refer to the SOL Employer Guide or contact the relevant supporting sector agency for your specific occupation for clarification on qualification requirements.Correctly explains the SOL job title selection process and additional requirements, but fails to confirm that KU Leuven is on the list of recognised institutions, which is a key point in the reference answer regarding how the university credential supports the EP application.
Question:
We're looking to hire a foreign specialist for a role that's on the Shortage Occupation List. Our candidate graduated from KU Leuven in Belgium. When we submit the EP application, do we just pick any job title, or is there a specific process we need to follow for the SOL role — and will the candidate's university help with the application?
Expected:
For a Shortage Occupation List (SOL) role, the SOL bonus recognises jobs requiring highly specialised skills that are in shortage in the local workforce, determined through a robust evaluation process considering industry needs and local workforce development efforts. To qualify for this bonus: (1) the candidate must perform the job duties listed for the specific shortage occupation; (2) you must select one of the eligible job titles in the EP application (not just any title); and (3) the candidate must meet checks on additional job requirements if they fall into certain categories. Regarding the candidate's university, Katholieke Universiteit Leuven (KU Leuven) in Belgium is on the list of recognised institutions, which can support the EP application (e.g., for meeting qualification-related criteria). So the university credential from KU Leuven is recognised, but you still need to follow the specific SOL application requirements including selecting the correct eligible job title and ensuring the candidate meets the relevant job duty and additional requirement checks.
Actual:
For an SOL role, you cannot simply pick any job title—there is a specific process you must follow:

**Job Title Selection:**
You must select one of the **eligible job titles** listed for the specific shortage occupation in your EP application form. This job title must match the occupation advertised on MyCareersFuture, unless you are exempted from the job advertising requirement. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md]

**Additional Requirements:**
Your candidate must meet the additional requirements specific to that shortage occupation, which can include:
- Relevant qualifications (uploaded in the qualifications section)
- Work experience in relevant job titles (selected in the work experience section)
- Registration proof (uploaded in the agency support question) [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md]

The candidate must also perform the actual job duties listed for that specific shortage occupation. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]

**Regarding the university:** The knowledge base does not contain information about whether the candidate's university (KU Leuven) plays a role in the application process. You should refer to the SOL Employer Guide or contact the relevant supporting sector agency for your specific occupation for clarification on qualification requirements.
Judge remark (scored 2):
Correctly explains the SOL job title selection process and additional requirements, but fails to confirm that KU Leuven is on the list of recognised institutions, which is a key point in the reference answer regarding how the university credential supports the EP application.

q-0030complex3We're looking to bring in a candidate on an EP for a Software Development Manager role. He doesn't have a degree in computer science or any related IT field — he studied mechanical engineering — but he does have about four years of experience as a Software Development Manager. Separately, we're also considering hiring a Relationship Manager for our family office private banking unit (we're a licensed FI with MAS). That candidate has two years of experience as a Relationship Manager in ultra-high net worth. Would both of these candidates qualify for the Skills Bonus under the shortage occupation list?Based on the COMPASS shortage occupation list, here's the assessment: **Software Development Manager:** Yes, this candidate qualifies. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/upcoming-changes-to-employment-pass-eligibility/complementarity-assessment-framework-compass/compass-shortage-occupation-list.md] The role accepts candidates with **either** a relevant Bachelor's degree **or** a minimum of three years' work experience in the job title. Your candidate has four years as a Software Development Manager, which exceeds the three-year requirement, so the lack of a computer science degree is not a barrier. **Relationship Manager (ultra-high/high net worth, family office or philanthropy):** No, this candidate does **not** qualify. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/upcoming-changes-to-employment-pass-eligibility/complementarity-assessment-framework-compass/compass-shortage-occupation-list.md] The role requires candidates to meet **both** criteria: (1) your employer is a Singapore licensed FI with private banking business (which you meet), and (2) **minimum total three years' work experience** in the role. Question:
We're looking to bring in a candidate on an EP for a Software Development Manager role. He doesn't have a degree in computer science or any related IT field — he studied mechanical engineering — but he does have about four years of experience as a Software Development Manager. Separately, we're also considering hiring a Relationship Manager for our family office private banking unit (we're a licensed FI with MAS). That candidate has two years of experience as a Relationship Manager in ultra-high net worth. Would both of these candidates qualify for the Skills Bonus under the shortage occupation list?
Expected:
For the Software Development Manager role, the candidate can qualify for the C5 Skills Bonus on the Shortage Occupation List even without a relevant degree, because the additional requirements allow either (1a) at least a Bachelor's degree in Computer Science, Information Technology, Programming & Systems Analysis, or Science (Computer Studies), OR (1b) a minimum total of three years' work experience in at least one of the eligible job titles (Application development manager, Head of applications development, Software and applications manager, or Software development manager). Since your candidate has four years of experience as a Software Development Manager, he satisfies criterion (1b) and would be eligible. The supporting agency is IMDA.

For the Relationship Manager (ultra-high/high net worth / family office / philanthropy) role, the candidate would NOT qualify. The additional requirements for that role require the candidate to meet BOTH criteria: (1) the employer must be a Singapore licensed FI with private banking business (which your firm satisfies — MOM verifies this directly with MAS, no additional documentation needed), AND (2) the candidate must have a minimum total of three years' work experience as a Relationship Manager (ultra-high/high net worth / family office / philanthropy). Your candidate has only two years of experience, which falls short of the three-year minimum. The supporting agency is MAS.

So only the Software Development Manager candidate would qualify; the Relationship Manager candidate would not.

Your candidate has four years as a Software Development Manager, which exceeds the three-year requirement, so the lack of a computer science degree is not a barrier.

**Relationship Manager (ultra-high/high net worth, family office or philanthropy):** No, this candidate does **not** qualify. Question:
We're a high-growth startup incorporated in Singapore. We've raised about USD 12 million over the last 3 years from a recognized VC firm (not EDBI or SEEDS Capital). We want to know if we'd qualify for the COMPASS C6 SEP bonus, and if so, which agency should we reach out to for confirmation?
Expected:
Yes, your startup could be eligible for the C6 Strategic Economic Priorities (SEP) bonus. To qualify under this programme (S/N 8 in the eligible programmes list), your organisation must be a high-growth startup that is incorporated in Singapore with a minimum investment amount of USD 10 million over the past 36 months, and must have received funding from EDBI, SEEDS Capital, or other recognised investment firms. Since your startup has raised USD 12 million over the last 3 years from a recognised investment firm, you would meet the investment threshold and funding source criteria. Your organisation needs to participate in at least one of the eligible SEP programmes to be considered for the C6 SEP bonus. The supporting agency for this programme is Enterprise Singapore (EnterpriseSG), and you should contact them directly if you have any queries about your eligibility.

q-0032complex3We want to appoint one of our EP holders as a director on the board of a subsidiary company we own. After we get MOM's approval, what do we need to do next, and when the EP comes up for renewal, do we need to do anything about the directorship approval as well?Based on the knowledge base, here's what you need to do: **After MOM approval:** Once you receive the Letter of Consent (LOC) from MOM, you need to [register the directorship position with ACRA](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/taking-up-secondary-directorship/index.md). **Upon EP renewal:** Yes, you will need to take action. [When you have successfully renewed the EP, you must apply for a new LOC](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/taking-up-secondary-directorship/index.md). The LOC's validity is limited to the expiry or cancellation date of the current EP, so it does not automatically carry over to the renewed pass.The chatbot's answer accurately covers both key points: registering the directorship with ACRA after receiving the LOC, and the need to apply for a new LOC upon EP renewal since the LOC validity is tied to the current EP's expiry date.
Question:
We want to appoint one of our EP holders as a director on the board of a subsidiary company we own. After we get MOM's approval, what do we need to do next, and when the EP comes up for renewal, do we need to do anything about the directorship approval as well?
Expected:
After MOM grants the Letter of Consent (LOC), you must register the directorship position with ACRA. MOM will generally grant the LOC for directorship appointments in companies that are related by corporate shareholding to the EP holder's primary employer, as these appointments may be critical to their primary job role. Regarding renewal: the LOC is valid only up to the expiry or cancellation date of the current EP, so when you successfully renew the EP, you must apply for a new LOC for the directorship to continue.
Actual:
Based on the knowledge base, here's what you need to do:

**After MOM approval:**
Once you receive the Letter of Consent (LOC) from MOM, you need to [register the directorship position with ACRA](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/taking-up-secondary-directorship/index.md).

**Upon EP renewal:**
Yes, you will need to take action. [When you have successfully renewed the EP, you must apply for a new LOC](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/taking-up-secondary-directorship/index.md). The LOC's validity is limited to the expiry or cancellation date of the current EP, so it does not automatically carry over to the renewed pass.

We have a ONE Pass holder who has changed employers and taken on a new occupation. I need to notify MOM of these changes. Can you tell me what employment-related information and personal particulars I'll need to submit, and whether there are any occupation restrictions I should check before proceeding?
Expected:
When notifying MOM of changes for a ONE Pass holder, you need to provide the following employment details: employer(s) and occupation(s), total annual income for the past calendar year, and other professional activities if applicable. You also need to submit personal particulars including: name, nationality/citizenship, date of birth, marital status, sex, country/region of birth or origin, and Malaysian ID details (if applicable), along with any required supporting documents for these fields. Additionally, before proceeding, you should check the full list of restricted occupations for the ONE Pass (available on MOM's website) to ensure the new occupation is not restricted.
