q-0034complex1I'm a researcher considering applying for the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass. I know it covers multiple sectors, but I'm not sure how the eligibility assessment works — do I just need to meet some general criteria, or is it more specific than that? And what kind of employer-related financial documents would I need to prepare, and how recent do they have to be?# Eligibility Assessment and Documentation for Researchers ## How Eligibility Assessment Works The assessment is **specific to your situation**, not just general criteria. You must meet **one of these routes** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md]: 1. **Salary-based route**: Earn a fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 for 12 consecutive months leading up to your application date, working for an established company (market cap ≥US$500M or annual revenue ≥US$200M). 2. **Outstanding achievements route**: If you don't meet the salary requirement, you may still qualify through outstanding achievements in academia and research (among other sectors). MOM and relevant agencies like MOE, NRF, and A*STAR will holistically review your work and key accomplishments [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md]. ## Employer Financial Documentation The knowledge base does not specify which employer-related financial documents you need to prepare or their recency requirements. I don't have enough information to answer that part of your question from the knowledge base. I recommend contacting MOM directly or consulting the [documents required page](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/documents-required) for a detailed checklist.Correctly explains the eligibility assessment is specific (category-based) and describes the two routes, but fails to provide the employer-related financial document requirements (latest financial info within 3 months, market cap/revenue documents, other income source documentation, employment verification) which the reference answer identifies as essential.
Question:
I'm a researcher considering applying for the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass. I know it covers multiple sectors, but I'm not sure how the eligibility assessment works — do I just need to meet some general criteria, or is it more specific than that? And what kind of employer-related financial documents would I need to prepare, and how recent do they have to be?
Expected:
The Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass covers top talent in all sectors, including academia and research, so you would be eligible to apply as a researcher. For eligibility, candidates are assessed on a case-by-case basis. You must choose the category that best fits you and meet all the criteria within that chosen category — not just some general criteria. Regarding employer-related financial documents, you need to provide the latest financial information of your employer, obtained within the last 3 months. This includes documents indicating the latest market capitalisation or annual company revenue. Additionally, if you have income sources other than your fixed monthly salary, you must provide documents supporting those other income sources. You will also need employment verification proof from a background screening company.
Actual:
# Eligibility Assessment and Documentation for Researchers

## How Eligibility Assessment Works

The assessment is **specific to your situation**, not just general criteria. You must meet **one of these routes** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md]:

1. **Salary-based route**: Earn a fixed monthly salary of at least S$30,000 for 12 consecutive months leading up to your application date, working for an established company (market cap ≥US$500M or annual revenue ≥US$200M).

2. **Outstanding achievements route**: If you don't meet the salary requirement, you may still qualify through outstanding achievements in academia and research (among other sectors). MOM and relevant agencies like MOE, NRF, and A*STAR will holistically review your work and key accomplishments [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/eligibility.md].

## Employer Financial Documentation

The knowledge base does not specify which employer-related financial documents you need to prepare or their recency requirements. I don't have enough information to answer that part of your question from the knowledge base. I recommend contacting MOM directly or consulting the [documents required page](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/documents-required) for a detailed checklist.

Question:
We have an ONE Pass holder whose common-law partner wants to work here in Singapore, and the pass holder also wants to bring over their 22-year-old unmarried son who has a disability, plus their 19-year-old stepchild. Can you walk me through what passes each of these family members would get, and whether any of them can work?
Expected:
The common-law spouse (whose common-law relationship is recognised under the laws of their country or region) would be issued a Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP) and can apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC) to work in Singapore. The 22-year-old unmarried handicapped son would also be eligible for a Long-Term Visit Pass, as handicapped children who are unmarried and aged 21 and above qualify for an LTVP. The 19-year-old unmarried stepchild would likewise qualify for a Long-Term Visit Pass, since stepchildren who are unmarried and under 21 years old are eligible for an LTVP. Note that the LTVP for the handicapped son and the stepchild does not mention eligibility for a Letter of Consent to work — only spouses and common-law spouses on their respective passes are described as being able to apply for an LOC to work in Singapore.
Actual:
Here's what each family member can get:

**Common-law partner:** A [Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP)][source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/index.md]. They can work in Singapore by applying for a Letter of Consent (LOC) — the employer completes the application on their behalf [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md].

**22-year-old unmarried son with disability:** An LTVP [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/index.md]. However, I don't have information in the knowledge base about whether disabled adult children on LTVP can work or apply for a Letter of Consent.

**19-year-old stepchild:** An LTVP [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/index.md]. Like other LTVP holders who are not common-law spouses, they would need to obtain a separate work pass (Employment Pass, S Pass, or Work Permit) if they want to work — they cannot work on the LTVP alone [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md].

Question:
We're filing an LTVP application for a ONE Pass holder's spouse who wants to work in Singapore. The spouse has support from the Singapore Medical Council, and most of their documents — including the SMC support letter — are in Mandarin. Also, the spouse previously entered Singapore on a passport from a different country than the one they're using now. How should we handle all of this on the application?
Expected:
There are several things to address in this application: (1) Since the applicant has obtained support from the Singapore Medical Council, which is one of the listed Vetting Agencies on the form, you must select 'Yes' to indicate vetting agency support and select 'Singapore Medical Council' from the list. You must also produce documentary proof of support from the Singapore Medical Council together with the application. (2) All non-English documents must be accompanied by an English translation, and the translation can be done by a translation service provider. So the Mandarin documents, including the SMC support letter if it is in Mandarin, need to have English translations attached. (3) In Part 5 – Other Information of the application form, the applicant must declare 'Yes' to the question asking whether they have entered Singapore using a passport issued by a different country, since that applies to their situation. Additionally, by submitting the application, the applicant consents to the Government of Singapore and statutory authorities obtaining from and verifying information with any person, organisation, or any other source.
Actual:
Based on the available CONTEXT, I can address one of your questions:

**Document translations:** Non-English documents must be accompanied by an English translation. The translation can be done by a translation service provider [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/work-permit-for-foreign-domestic-worker/apply-for-work-permit/fdw-appln-form.md]. This requirement applies to work pass applications generally, so your Mandarin documents—including the SMC support letter—should be translated to English.

However, I don't have enough information in the knowledge base to answer:

- How to indicate professional credentials or support letters (like the SMC letter) on the LTVP application form
- Where to declare previous entries to Singapore on a different passport

For these LTVP-specific questions, you would need to contact MOM directly or consult the detailed LTVP application instructions, which are not included in the available excerpts.

Question:
My company is terminating an EP holder and their last day is 15 February. We want to submit the cancellation early, and we also want to make sure the person can stay in Singapore for a while after the pass is cancelled to settle personal matters. Also, if MOM asks us for extra documents during the process, how do we send those in? And one more thing — after cancellation, if the person still has their physical EP card, what should we do with it?
Expected:
You can submit the cancellation request up to 14 days in advance — so for a 15 February cancellation, you can submit from 1 February onwards. The cancellation is immediate unless it is an advance cancellation request. The employer or employment agent can submit the cancellation. Upon cancellation (or within a day after the EP expiration date), you can request a Short-Term Visit Pass (STVP) for the pass holder, which grants valid stay in Singapore for up to 90 days. If MOM emails you requesting more documents, you can submit them using the online form at https://form.gov.sg/#!/5f8d4eadef8dab00112fd313. Regarding the physical EP card, if the card is found (or still in possession), it should be returned to MOM immediately by post to: Ministry of Manpower, 18 Havelock Road, Singapore 059764.
Actual:

**Timing of cancellation submission:**
You can submit your EP cancellation request up to 14 days in advance. Since the last day is 15 February, you can submit from 1 February onwards. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/cancel-a-pass.md]

**Extending stay after cancellation:**
You can request an STVP (Short-Term Visit Pass) for the pass holder upon cancellation. The STVP will grant them valid stay in Singapore for up to 90 days from the date of application, allowing them time to settle personal matters before departure. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/cancel-a-pass.md]

**Physical EP card:**
After cancellation, you should cut the card in half and discard it to prevent misuse. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/cancel-a-pass.md]

**Regarding additional documents:**
I don't have enough information from the knowledge base about the process for submitting additional documents if MOM requests them during the EP cancellation process. You may need to contact MOM directly or check the EP eService portal for guidance on document submission procedures.

Question:
We have a candidate currently based overseas whom we're looking to bring in on a ONE Pass. If the main application gets rejected, what happens to the dependant pass applications we submitted at the same time — do we get the fees back? And once the candidate is here and the pass is issued, is there an annual reporting obligation they need to be aware of right away, or is there some grace period?
Expected:
If the main ONE Pass holder's application is rejected, the family's dependant pass applications will also be rejected, and the fees paid for all of those applications are non-refundable. The candidate can apply for the pass while outside Singapore, but if approved they must make an appointment to get the pass issued at the Employment Pass Services Centre in Singapore. Once the pass is issued, ONE Pass holders are generally required to submit an annual notification, but there are exceptions — for example, those who have just been issued their pass are not required to submit the annual notification right away.

q-0039complex3I'm going through the Employment Pass application process and I want to understand the full picture — if MOM asks me for additional documents, how do I submit them, and what kind of obligations or commitments does my employer (or I) take on as part of this application, especially regarding compliance and repatriation costs?# Submitting Additional Documents If MOM requests additional documents during your Employment Pass application, you have two options [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/apply-for-a-pass/index.md]: 1. **Submit via the online portal**: Log into the Employment Pass eService, click 'Work Passes' on the left menu, choose your application record, and select 'Submit documents'. 2. **Use the online form**: MOM will email you instructions to submit documents using their [online form](https://form.gov.sg/#!/5f8d4eadef8dab00112fd313). # Employer's Obligations and Commitments Your employer (the local sponsor) takes on several key responsibilities upon approval [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/apply-for-a-pass/ep_sponsor_form8.md]: - **Responsibility for your stay, maintenance, and repatriation** - **Financial indemnity**: The employer indemnifies the Singapore Government for any charges or expenses incurred in repatriating you or your dependants - **Compliance oversight**: The employer is responsible for ensuring you comply with any quarantine and medical surveillance requirements - **Employment restrictions**: You can only work in the role specified in the application; any change in duties, designation, or employer requires notification to MOM or a new work pass application - **Timely cancellation**: The employer must cancel your Employment Pass within 7 days of employment terminationThe chatbot accurately covers both document submission methods (portal and online form) and comprehensively addresses employer obligations including repatriation indemnity, compliance requirements, and additional relevant details like employment restrictions and cancellation timelines that enhance the answer.
Question:
I'm going through the Employment Pass application process and I want to understand the full picture — if MOM asks me for additional documents, how do I submit them, and what kind of obligations or commitments does my employer (or I) take on as part of this application, especially regarding compliance and repatriation costs?
Expected:
If MOM requests additional documents during processing (which may take longer than 10 working days), you can submit them via the Employment Pass e-Service by clicking 'Work Passes' on the left menu, choosing the application record, and selecting 'Submit documents' (or selecting 'View details' to see the required action). Alternatively, MOM's email may direct you to submit documents using their online form. As part of the application, a declaration is made confirming that the applicant has read, understood, and will comply with all the Conditions of Employment Pass as specified in the Employment of Foreign Manpower (Work Passes) Regulations available on MOM's website, and will ensure these conditions are complied with. Additionally, there is an obligation to indemnify the Singapore Government for any charges or expenses which may be incurred by the Government in respect of the repatriation of the candidate or any of his/her dependants.
Actual:
# Submitting Additional Documents

If MOM requests additional documents during your Employment Pass application, you have two options [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/apply-for-a-pass/index.md]:

1. **Submit via the online portal**: Log into the Employment Pass eService, click 'Work Passes' on the left menu, choose your application record, and select 'Submit documents'.
2. **Use the online form**: MOM will email you instructions to submit documents using their [online form](https://form.gov.sg/#!/5f8d4eadef8dab00112fd313).

# Employer's Obligations and Commitments

Your employer (the local sponsor) takes on several key responsibilities upon approval [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/apply-for-a-pass/ep_sponsor_form8.md]:

- **Responsibility for your stay, maintenance, and repatriation**
- **Financial indemnity**: The employer indemnifies the Singapore Government for any charges or expenses incurred in repatriating you or your dependants
- **Compliance oversight**: The employer is responsible for ensuring you comply with any quarantine and medical surveillance requirements
- **Employment restrictions**: You can only work in the role specified in the application; any change in duties, designation, or employer requires notification to MOM or a new work pass application
- **Timely cancellation**: The employer must cancel your Employment Pass within 7 days of employment termination

Question:
We're bringing in a senior hire on a ONE Pass, and the candidate wants to understand what they're committing to when they sign the application. Specifically, they're asking whether they can freely take up any role once the pass is issued, and whether there are any conduct-related undertakings they need to be aware of during their stay. Can you clarify what obligations they'd be agreeing to?
Expected:
When signing the ONE Pass application, the candidate makes several undertakings. First, they declare that all information provided in the application and all supporting documents submitted are true and correct, and that the documents are true copies of the originals. Second, regarding conduct during their stay in Singapore, they undertake not to misuse controlled drugs and not to take part in any political or other activities that would make them an undesirable or prohibited immigrant under the Immigration Act. Third, regarding employment, they cannot freely take up any role — they must obtain prior approval from the Ministry of Manpower if they intend to start working in a restricted occupation specified by the Ministry after the ONE Pass is issued to them.

q-0041complex3We're submitting an EP application and need to verify the candidate's degree from NTU through their online verification portal. I want to make sure we get the screenshots right and also handle the MOM verification reference number properly. What exactly do we need to capture and upload for the educational qualification verification, and how does the MOM reference number fit into the process?# Educational Qualification Verification for EP Application When verifying an NTU degree through their online verification portal, you need to capture **two separate screenshots** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/verification-portal-guide.md § Page 3]: **Screenshot 1 – Verification Page:** - Visible verification portal website address - All information entered in the portal, including: - Name - Date of birth **Screenshot 2 – Verified Page:** - Visible verification portal website address - Final page showing proof that the candidate was awarded the educational qualification Both screenshots must then be uploaded to your EP application form [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/verification-portal-guide.md § Page 2]. ## MOM Verification Reference Number The MOM verification reference number applies to a **different verification method** — when using a Background Screening Company rather than the institution's portal. If you're using NTU's direct verification portal, you won't have an MOM reference number to enter. The MOM reference number is only relevant if you obtain verification proof from a Background Screening Company, which you would then key into the application form [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/bsc-guide.md § Page 3].The chatbot accurately describes both required screenshots with all essential details (visible website address, entered information, final verification page), correctly explains the MOM reference number applies to background screening company verification rather than direct portal verification, and provides clear guidance. The passport/MRZ requirement mentioned in the reference is supplementary general guidance not specifically asked about.
Question:
We're submitting an EP application and need to verify the candidate's degree from NTU through their online verification portal. I want to make sure we get the screenshots right and also handle the MOM verification reference number properly. What exactly do we need to capture and upload for the educational qualification verification, and how does the MOM reference number fit into the process?
Expected:
For verifying the candidate's educational qualification through a university verification portal (e.g., NTU), you need to capture and upload two screenshots: (1) A 'Verification page' screenshot that must include the visible website address of the verification portal and all information entered in the portal, such as the candidate's name and date of birth. (2) A 'Verified page' screenshot that must include the visible website address of the verification portal and the final page showing that the candidate was awarded the educational qualification. Both the verification page and verified page screenshots should be uploaded. Separately, when dealing with the MOM verification reference number, you need to key in the 'MOM verification reference number' as stated on the verification proof or as provided by the background screening company, and then upload the verification proof from the background screening company. Additionally, when uploading any documents such as passport pages, you must ensure the whole page, including the entire 'Machine Readable Zone', is visible — it must not be cut off, blocked, or covered by a watermark.
Actual:
# Educational Qualification Verification for EP Application

When verifying an NTU degree through their online verification portal, you need to capture **two separate screenshots** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/verification-portal-guide.md § Page 3]:

**Screenshot 1 – Verification Page:**
- Visible verification portal website address
- All information entered in the portal, including:
  - Name
  - Date of birth

**Screenshot 2 – Verified Page:**
- Visible verification portal website address
- Final page showing proof that the candidate was awarded the educational qualification

Both screenshots must then be uploaded to your EP application form [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/verification-portal-guide.md § Page 2].

## MOM Verification Reference Number

The MOM verification reference number applies to a **different verification method** — when using a Background Screening Company rather than the institution's portal. If you're using NTU's direct verification portal, you won't have an MOM reference number to enter. The MOM reference number is only relevant if you obtain verification proof from a Background Screening Company, which you would then key into the application form [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/bsc-guide.md § Page 3].
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot accurately describes both required screenshots with all essential details (visible website address, entered information, final verification page), correctly explains the MOM reference number applies to background screening company verification rather than direct portal verification, and provides clear guidance. The passport/MRZ requirement mentioned in the reference is supplementary general guidance not specifically asked about.

q-0042complex3I'm on an Employment Pass under the Sponsorship scheme and my pass still has about 4 months left. I want to renew early — will I lose those remaining months? Also, what form do I need to use, and what kind of obligations or commitments does the renewal involve regarding repatriation?No, you will not lose your remaining months. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md] When you renew early, any remaining validity from your current pass (up to six months) is added on to your renewal, so the duration of the renewed pass will start when your current pass expires. For EP (Sponsorship) renewals, you must use the **Employment Pass (Sponsorship) Renewal Form**. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md] You can start applying up to 3 months before your pass expires. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/ep_sponsor_renewal_form.md] Download and complete the form as a soft copy, then submit it along with supporting documents via the online form at go.gov.sg/renew-ep-sponsorship. Processing typically takes around 8 weeks. Regarding repatriation obligations, as the local sponsor, you undertake to: [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/ep_sponsor_renewal_form.md] - Be responsible for the stay, maintenance, and repatriation of the candidate - Indemnify the Singapore Government for any charges or expenses incurred in repatriating the candidate or their dependants - Cancel the candidate's Employment Pass within 7 days upon termination of employmentThe chatbot accurately addresses all three parts of the question: confirms remaining months carry over (up to 6 months), identifies the correct EP Sponsorship Renewal Form, and explains repatriation obligations including the indemnification requirement. It provides additional helpful details like processing time and submission method without introducing errors.
Question:
I'm on an Employment Pass under the Sponsorship scheme and my pass still has about 4 months left. I want to renew early — will I lose those remaining months? Also, what form do I need to use, and what kind of obligations or commitments does the renewal involve regarding repatriation?
Expected:
If you renew your Employment Pass early, your new work pass validity will not be reduced or lost. Any remaining validity from your current pass — up to six months — is added on to your renewal, so your 4 remaining months would carry over. However, if you miss the renewal deadline, you would need to apply for a new pass instead. As an EP (Sponsorship) holder, you must use the specific EP Sponsorship renewal form for your renewal application. The form requires the candidate's name as shown on their travel document, their signature, and the date in DD/MM/YYYY format. As part of the renewal, there is an obligation to indemnify the Singapore Government for any charges or expenses that may be incurred by the Government in respect of the repatriation of the candidate or any of his/her dependants.
Actual:
No, you will not lose your remaining months. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md] When you renew early, any remaining validity from your current pass (up to six months) is added on to your renewal, so the duration of the renewed pass will start when your current pass expires.

For EP (Sponsorship) renewals, you must use the **Employment Pass (Sponsorship) Renewal Form**. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md] You can start applying up to 3 months before your pass expires. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/ep_sponsor_renewal_form.md] Download and complete the form as a soft copy, then submit it along with supporting documents via the online form at go.gov.sg/renew-ep-sponsorship. Processing typically takes around 8 weeks.

Regarding repatriation obligations, as the local sponsor, you undertake to: [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/ep_sponsor_renewal_form.md]

- Be responsible for the stay, maintenance, and repatriation of the candidate
- Indemnify the Singapore Government for any charges or expenses incurred in repatriating the candidate or their dependants
- Cancel the candidate's Employment Pass within 7 days upon termination of employment

q-0043complex3We have an ONE Pass holder whose spouse holds a Dependant's Pass and now wants to start her own business in Singapore. The DP currently has about 2 months of validity left. Can we go ahead and submit the Letter of Consent application for her, and who should be the one submitting it — us as the employer, or can she do it herself?Based on the knowledge base, there are two issues to address: **DP Validity:** You cannot proceed with the Letter of Consent application yet. The DP must have at least 3 months validity remaining, and your spouse's pass currently has only 2 months left. [You will need to renew the DP first before applying for the Letter of Consent.](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore) **Who Should Submit:** Since your spouse wants to operate a business (rather than work for an employer), she or an appointed employment agent can submit the application herself—you as an employer do not need to be involved. [The candidate or appointed employment agent can apply if the candidate wishes to operate a business in Singapore.](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore) Once the DP is renewed to have sufficient validity, the Letter of Consent application typically takes within 4 weeks to process and is free of charge.The chatbot correctly addresses both key points: the DP needs at least 3 months validity (so renewal is required first), and for operating a business, the candidate herself or an employment agent can apply rather than the employer. Question:
We have an ONE Pass holder whose spouse holds a Dependant's Pass and now wants to start her own business in Singapore. The DP currently has about 2 months of validity left. Can we go ahead and submit the Letter of Consent application for her, and who should be the one submitting it — us as the employer, or can she do it herself?
Expected:
No, you cannot proceed with the application immediately. The Dependant's Pass must have at least 3 months of validity remaining, so since it only has about 2 months left, the DP needs to be renewed first before submitting the Letter of Consent (LOC) application. As for who submits it: because the spouse wishes to operate a business in Singapore (rather than work for an employer), the candidate herself or an appointed employment agent can apply — the employer does not need to submit it on her behalf. If she instead wanted to work for an employer in Singapore, then the employer would need to apply on her behalf. The LOC is free of charge, processing takes within 4 weeks for most cases, and its duration is up to the expiry date of the Dependant's Pass.
Actual:

**DP Validity:** You cannot proceed with the Letter of Consent application yet. The DP must have at least 3 months validity remaining, and your spouse's pass currently has only 2 months left. [You will need to renew the DP first before applying for the Letter of Consent.](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore)

**Who Should Submit:** Since your spouse wants to operate a business (rather than work for an employer), she or an appointed employment agent can submit the application herself—you as an employer do not need to be involved. [The candidate or appointed employment agent can apply if the candidate wishes to operate a business in Singapore.](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore)

Once the DP is renewed to have sufficient validity, the Letter of Consent application typically takes within 4 weeks to process and is free of charge.

Additionally, MOM may call you for an interview before issuing a new card or if you lose your card repeatedly.

q-0045hard-11We're bringing in a foreign candidate on an EP, and as part of his role he'll also need to sit on the board of one of our subsidiary companies that we hold shares in. I want to make sure I understand what's needed — does he need a separate work pass for that directorship, and when I'm submitting his EP application, how should I handle the educational qualification entry if his university isn't showing up in the dropdown list?Based on the knowledge base, here's what you need to know: **Regarding the directorship role:** Yes, your candidate will need a separate authorization for the directorship. Even though he's an EP holder, taking up a directorship position constitutes work under the Companies Act and requires a [Letter of Consent (LOC)](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/taking-up-secondary-directorship/index.md). However, since the subsidiary is related to your company by shareholding, MOM will generally grant the LOC. The process takes within 5 weeks, and you'll need to submit his appointment letter to the directorship role [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/taking-up-secondary-directorship/index.md]. You can apply for the LOC after his EP is approved. **Regarding the educational qualification:** I don't have enough information to answer that from the knowledge base. Question:
We're bringing in a foreign candidate on an EP, and as part of his role he'll also need to sit on the board of one of our subsidiary companies that we hold shares in. I want to make sure I understand what's needed — does he need a separate work pass for that directorship, and when I'm submitting his EP application, how should I handle the educational qualification entry if his university isn't showing up in the dropdown list?
Expected:
For the secondary directorship: EP holders are generally only allowed to work for their employer as specified on their work pass. Taking up a directorship appointment (executive or non-executive) in another company constitutes a form of work under the Companies Act, so a work pass is required. The EP holder must apply for and be granted a Letter of Consent (LOC) under regulation 11 of the Employment of Foreign Manpower (Work Passes) Regulations 2012. MOM will generally grant an LOC to allow EP holders to take up secondary directorship appointments in companies that are related by corporate shareholding to the EP holder's primary employer, as these appointments may be critical to their primary job role. Since your subsidiary is related by corporate shareholding, MOM would generally grant the LOC.

For the educational qualification in the EP application: If the institution name on the educational certificate or verification proof is not available in the dropdown menu, you should enter the institution's most recent name where applicable. Alternatively, you may choose not to declare the qualification if you are unable to find it on the list. You should also enter the qualification level as per the verification proof or educational certificate, and enter the faculty as per the transcript if it is not found in the verification proof or educational certificate.

However, since the subsidiary is related to your company by shareholding, MOM will generally grant the LOC. Question:
I currently hold an Employment Pass but I'm thinking of switching to the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass. If my ONE Pass application gets rejected, will MOM just convert it into an EP application automatically? Also, if I do get the ONE Pass, how do I go about cancelling my old pass — can I do it myself, and how long does the cancellation take?
Expected:

Your employer should submit a separate application for the Employment Pass if you meet its eligibility requirements. Regarding cancellation of the pass (e.g., if you no longer plan to work in Singapore): you should cancel within 1 week before your departure date (for example, if your departure date is 10 February, submit the request from 3 February onwards). Either you or your employment agent can cancel the pass, and the cancellation is immediate.
Actual:
No, if your ONE Pass application is rejected, you will not be automatically assessed for an Employment Pass. Your employer would need to [submit a separate application for the Employment Pass](https://www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/apply-for-a-pass) if you meet its eligibility requirements [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/faq/one-pass/if-my-one-pass-application-is-unsuccessful-will-i-be-automatically-assessed-for-an-employment-pass.md].

Instead, ask your current employer to cancel your EP only **after** your ONE Pass application is approved and when you are ready to get the pass issued [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/faq/one-pass/should-i-cancel-my-employment-pass-if-i-want-to-apply-for-the-overseas-networks-and-expertise-pass.md]. Question:
We're bringing in a candidate on the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass. He has a 22-year-old unmarried son who he'd like to bring along to Singapore. Can we get a dependent pass for the son, and separately, once the candidate's own ONE Pass is approved in-principle, what payment methods are accepted when we go down to have the pass issued, and are there any ongoing obligations around updating MOM on address changes after issuance?
Expected:
The 22-year-old son is not eligible for a family/dependent pass under the ONE Pass, because passes for family are not available for children who are married or over the age of 21. If the son wishes to work in Singapore, he would need to obtain his own work pass. For the candidate's own ONE Pass issuance, an appointment must be made at the Employment Pass Services Centre, and the required supporting documents must be brought along. Payment of fees can be made using VISA, MasterCard, AMEX, NETS, or CashCard; if the credit or debit card was not issued in Singapore, it must be activated for overseas use. After issuance, the pass holder is obligated to notify the Work Pass Division, Ministry of Manpower within 14 days of any change in contact details, including residential address.

Since the son is 22, he would need to obtain his own work pass if he wishes to work in Singapore.

I don't have enough information to answer your questions about payment methods accepted when the pass is issued or the specific procedures for updating address changes after issuance. q-0048hard-12My partner and I are in a common-law relationship that's recognised in our home country, and I'm on a Long-Term Visit Pass tied to an ONE Pass holder. I want to work in Singapore — do I need to get a separate work pass like an Employment Pass, or is there another way? And if I apply through my partner's employer, how long can the pass duration be?Based on your situation, you have a streamlined option available to you. Since you're on a Long-Term Visit Pass as a common-law spouse of an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder, you can apply for a **Letter of Consent (LOC)** to work in Singapore without needing a separate work pass like an Employment Pass [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md]. Your partner's employer can submit the LOC application on your behalf by downloading and completing the Letter of Consent (Common-Law Spouse) application form and submitting it with supporting documents online [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md]. Regarding duration, the LOC is valid **up to the expiry date of your Long-Term Visit Pass** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore.md]. Question:
My partner and I are in a common-law relationship that's recognised in our home country, and I'm on a Long-Term Visit Pass tied to an ONE Pass holder. I want to work in Singapore — do I need to get a separate work pass like an Employment Pass, or is there another way? And if I apply through my partner's employer, how long can the pass duration be?
Expected:
As a common-law spouse on a Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP) linked to an Overseas Networks & Expertise (ONE) Pass holder, you can apply for a Letter of Consent (LOC) to work in Singapore. You do not need to obtain a separate work pass such as an Employment Pass. This is specifically for common-law spouses — other LTVP holders who are not common-law spouses would need to obtain a work pass to work in Singapore. When applying for the LOC, the pass duration applied for can be up to 60 months, although if the application is approved, the period granted may be shorter than what is indicated in the application.
Actual:
Based on your situation, you have a streamlined option available to you.

Since you're on a Long-Term Visit Pass as a common-law spouse of an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass holder, you can apply for a **Letter of Consent (LOC)** to work in Singapore without needing a separate work pass like an Employment Pass [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md]. Your partner's employer can submit the LOC application on your behalf by downloading and completing the Letter of Consent (Common-Law Spouse) application form and submitting it with supporting documents online [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md].

Regarding duration, the LOC is valid **up to the expiry date of your Long-Term Visit Pass** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore.md]. q-0049hard-12We're preparing an EP application for a candidate whose university doesn't appear in MOM's list of recognized institutions. On top of that, the candidate's nationality is already well-represented among our PMET staff, and I'm also wondering how our local PMET hiring ratio factors in. Can you walk me through what we need to handle for the qualification verification and how these workforce composition factors play into the application outcome?I'll walk you through how these factors affect your EP application under COMPASS. ## Qualifications & Verification Since your candidate holds a degree-equivalent qualification from a university not on MOM's top-tier list, they earn **10 points** under the C2 Qualifications criterion [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md § C2. Qualifications]. You **must submit verification proof** of the candidate's qualifications together with your EP application [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md § C2. Qualifications > Verification proof requirements]. ## Workforce Composition Factors **Diversity (C3):** Since the candidate's nationality is already well-represented among your PMET staff, this will likely score **0 points**. If their nationality forms 25% or more of your organisation's PMETs, no points are awarded [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md § C3. Diversity]. **Support for Local Employment (C4):** Your organisation's local PMET hiring ratio is assessed relative to your sector benchmark. Depending on where your local PMET share falls, you can earn 0, 10, or 20 points—or a minimum of 10 points if your local PMET share is at least 70% [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md § C4. Support for local employment]. **Overall strategy:** With 10 points from qualifications and potential weakness in diversity, you'll need to compensate through strong salary benchmarking (C1) and/or your local employment support score to reach the required 40 points to pass COMPASS.Covers key points about verification proof requirement, nationality diversity impact, and local PMET share criterion, but omits the specific instruction to enter the institution name exactly as it appears on the certificate. Question:
We're preparing an EP application for a candidate whose university doesn't appear in MOM's list of recognized institutions. On top of that, the candidate's nationality is already well-represented among our PMET staff, and I'm also wondering how our local PMET hiring ratio factors in. Can you walk me through what we need to handle for the qualification verification and how these workforce composition factors play into the application outcome?
Expected:
For the qualification verification: if the candidate's awarding institution cannot be found on MOM's list, you must enter its name exactly as it appears on the educational certificate, and the employer must upload a verification proof of the qualification to confirm it is genuine and from an accredited institution.

Regarding workforce composition factors under the COMPASS framework, two distinct criteria are relevant:

1. Nationality diversity criterion: This criterion awards more points to applications where the candidate's nationality forms a small share of the firm's PMET employees. A diverse mix of nationalities enriches firms with new ideas and networks and contributes to a more inclusive and resilient workforce. Since the candidate's nationality is already well-represented among your PMET staff, this criterion would award fewer points for the application.

2. Local PMET share criterion: Points are also affected by whether your firm has a relatively higher share of locals among PMET employees compared to your peers in the same sector. A higher local share relative to sector peers would be favorable.
Actual:
I'll walk you through how these factors affect your EP application under COMPASS.

## Qualifications & Verification

Since your candidate holds a degree-equivalent qualification from a university not on MOM's top-tier list, they earn **10 points** under the C2 Qualifications criterion [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md § C2. Qualifications]. You **must submit verification proof** of the candidate's qualifications together with your EP application [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md § C2. Qualifications > Verification proof requirements].

## Workforce Composition Factors

**Diversity (C3):** Since the candidate's nationality is already well-represented among your PMET staff, this will likely score **0 points**. If their nationality forms 25% or more of your organisation's PMETs, no points are awarded [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md § C3. Diversity].

**Support for Local Employment (C4):** Your organisation's local PMET hiring ratio is assessed relative to your sector benchmark. Depending on where your local PMET share falls, you can earn 0, 10, or 20 points—or a minimum of 10 points if your local PMET share is at least 70% [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md § C4. Support for local employment].

**Overall strategy:** With 10 points from qualifications and potential weakness in diversity, you'll need to compensate through strong salary benchmarking (C1) and/or your local employment support score to reach the required 40 points to pass COMPASS.

Question:
I'm renewing my Employment Pass under a sponsorship arrangement. The HR team mentioned that certain employer contributions to provident funds shouldn't be counted as part of my salary, and they also want to know what company financial details they need to provide on the renewal form. Can you tell me what the renewal form asks about the sponsor company's financials, and confirm whether provident fund contributions count toward salary?
Expected:
Regarding the sponsor company's financials on the Employment Pass (Sponsorship) Renewal Form: the form requires the sponsor company's paid-up capital (in S$), the nature of business, and the company's turnover for the past 3 years. For each of the 3 years (starting with the most recent), the company must provide the year, the value of turnover (in S$), and indicate whether the turnover figure is from an audited account (selecting 'Yes' or 'No'; for unaudited accounts or if the employing company is exempted from audit, 'No' should be selected). Regarding provident fund contributions: any contributions payable by the employer to any pension or provident fund, including any contributions made on the foreign employee's behalf, are excluded from the computation of salary (i.e., they do not count as part of the foreign employee's fixed monthly salary).

Question:
We're looking to hire a clinical psychologist from overseas — the candidate is 38 years old. I know clinical psychology is on the Shortage Occupation List, so they'd get the skills bonus under COMPASS. But I want to understand what fixed salary we'd need to offer to score points on the COMPASS salary criterion as well. What's the salary benchmark at age 38 for both the 10-point and 20-point thresholds, and are there any specific qualification or registration requirements we need to meet for the SOL skills bonus for a clinical psychologist?
Expected:
For the COMPASS salary criterion at age 38, the required salary benchmarks are $8,530 to score 10 points (65th percentile of local PMETs) and $14,045 to score 20 points (90th percentile of local PMETs). These benchmarks are based on the candidate's age and represent the salary thresholds relative to local PMET pay in the sector.

For the Shortage Occupation List (SOL) skills bonus for a clinical psychologist, the eligible job title is 'Clinical psychologist'. The key job duties include assessing mental health and social needs, formulating care, and delivering a range of psychological therapies including Cognitive Behavioural Therapy across the age range and across hospital and community settings. More details on job duties can be found on MOH's Team Healthcare website.

The additional requirements are that applicants should have at least a Master's degree in Clinical Psychology. MOH will assess all applications and may contact employers for more information. The supporting sector agency is the Ministry of Health (MOH).

Question:
I'm about to renew an Employment Pass for one of our employees whose pass expires in October 2024. I also need to submit their educational qualification details as part of the application. Can you tell me what I need to know about the renewal criteria and how to handle the qualification entry if the institution name isn't showing up in the dropdown?
Expected:
For the renewal: Since the EP is expiring from 1 September 2024 onwards (which includes October 2024), the pass holder will need to pass the COMPASS requirements in order to renew.

For the educational qualification entry: If the institution name on the educational certificate or verification proof is not available in the dropdown menu, you should enter the institution's most recent name where applicable. You may also choose not to declare the qualification if you are unable to find it on the list. Additionally, you should enter the qualification level as per the verification proof or educational certificate, and enter the faculty as per the transcript if it is not found in the verification proof or educational certificate. You should also read the guidelines before submitting the verification proof.
Actual:
# Employment Pass Renewal: Criteria and Qualification Handling

## Renewal Criteria

For your October 2024 expiry, your employee must meet these requirements [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md]:

- Earn at least the EP qualifying salary
- Work for the same employer in a managerial, executive, or specialised job
- Pass the points-based COMPASS (Complementarity Assessment Framework) — unless they earn at least $22,500 fixed monthly salary, in which case they are exempted

You can start applying up to 6 months before expiry, and the renewed pass is valid for up to 3 years.

## Handling Qualifications Not in the Dropdown

If the awarding institution doesn't appear in the Self-Assessment Tool dropdown [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/apply-for-a-pass/ep-and-s-pass-candidate-form.md]:

- Enter the institution name exactly as it appears on the educational certificate
- Your employer must upload verification proof confirming the qualification is genuine and from an accredited institution
- Applications will be rejected if qualifications are doubtful or from unaccredited institutions

You can obtain verification proof from background screening companies listed on MOM's website. Note that once a qualification has been verified, it does not need to be verified again at renewal or if the employee changes employers [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md].

Question:
We're looking to bring in a process engineer for our semiconductor fab on an Employment Pass. The candidate has a Bachelor's in Chemical Engineering. If the EP application gets rejected, can the candidate himself write in to MOM to appeal, or does it have to come from us as the employer? Also, are there any other requirements beyond the engineering degree that MOM checks for this particular role?
Expected:
For the appeal: Only the employer or an authorised third party who submitted the EP application can make enquiries or appeals to MOM. MOM will not entertain enquiries or appeals from the candidate or anyone else, so the candidate cannot write in himself — it must come from you as the employer (or your authorised third party).

For the process engineer role under the shortage occupation list, the candidate must meet both of the following criteria:
(1) The candidate must be from a firm involved in the Semiconductor space. MOM verifies this directly with EDB, so no additional documentation is required from the employer. New entrants to the Semiconductor space can reach out to EDB.
(2) The candidate must have at least a Bachelor's Degree in an engineering or science faculty. This includes, but is not limited to: Electrical Engineering, Mechanical Engineering, Chemical Engineering, Materials Engineering, and Material Sciences.

A Bachelor's in Chemical Engineering satisfies criterion (2), but criterion (1) — that the firm is in the semiconductor space — must also be met, and MOM will verify this with EDB directly.

And separately, if my Employment Pass gets cancelled while I'm still in Singapore, how long can I stay on a Short-Term Visit Pass?I can answer part of your question, but I don't have complete information on both points. **Switching employers on the ONE Pass:** I don't have enough information in the knowledge base to answer whether you need to reapply for a new ONE Pass when changing employers. The available excerpts don't address this scenario. **Short-Term Visit Pass (STVP) duration:** If you're still in Singapore when your Employment Pass is cancelled, you can request an STVP through the EP eService on the myMOM Portal. The STVP grants you a valid stay for **up to 90 days from the date of application** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/cancel-a-pass.md]. You'll receive it by email and must present it along with a valid passport to the immigration officer when leaving Singapore. For clarification on employer changes under the ONE Pass, I'd recommend contacting MOM directly or consulting the full application guidelines.Correctly answered the STVP duration (up to 90 days), but failed to answer the key question about ONE Pass employer switching, which the reference states does not require reapplication and allows concurrent work for multiple companies.
Question:
I'm currently exploring my options between the ONE Pass and the Employment Pass. One thing I'm curious about — if I hold a ONE Pass and decide to switch to a different employer, do I need to reapply for a new pass? And separately, if my Employment Pass gets cancelled while I'm still in Singapore, how long can I stay on a Short-Term Visit Pass?
Expected:
If you hold a ONE Pass (Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass), you do not need to reapply for a pass if you change jobs. In fact, the ONE Pass provides the flexibility to concurrently start, operate, and work for multiple companies at any one time (subject to your employment contract terms, where relevant). It is also exempt from the Complementarity Assessment Framework (COMPASS) and Fair Consideration Framework job advertising requirements. Additionally, passes are available for eligible family members, and your spouse will be able to apply to work on a Letter of Consent. You may also apply for the ONE Pass without meeting the salary criteria if you have outstanding achievements in at least one specified area. Separately, for Employment Pass holders who are still in Singapore after their pass is cancelled, they can request a Short-Term Visit Pass (STVP) to grant them a valid stay for up to 90 days from the date of application.
Actual:
I can answer part of your question, but I don't have complete information on both points.

**Switching employers on the ONE Pass:**
I don't have enough information in the knowledge base to answer whether you need to reapply for a new ONE Pass when changing employers. The available excerpts don't address this scenario.

**Short-Term Visit Pass (STVP) duration:**
If you're still in Singapore when your Employment Pass is cancelled, you can request an STVP through the EP eService on the myMOM Portal. The STVP grants you a valid stay for **up to 90 days from the date of application** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/cancel-a-pass.md]. You'll receive it by email and must present it along with a valid passport to the immigration officer when leaving Singapore.

For clarification on employer changes under the ONE Pass, I'd recommend contacting MOM directly or consulting the full application guidelines.
Judge remark (scored 1):
Correctly answered the STVP duration (up to 90 days), but failed to answer the key question about ONE Pass employer switching, which the reference states does not require reapplication and allows concurrent work for multiple companies.

q-0055hard-12We have an ONE Pass holder whose common-law spouse currently holds a Long-Term Visit Pass. The spouse has now received a job offer and wants to work here. Also, the ONE Pass holder's legally married spouse is on a Dependant's Pass and has also been offered a role — but she's actually been selected for an EP. Do both spouses need the same type of authorization to start working, and what do we need to do with the DP before the EP is issued?No, the two spouses do not need the same type of authorization, and there are different steps required for each. **For the common-law spouse (on Long-Term Visit Pass):** They need a Letter of Consent (LOC) to work in Singapore. The prospective employer must download and submit the LOC (Common-Law Spouse) application form with supporting documents on their behalf. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md] **For the legally married spouse (on Dependant's Pass):** Since she has been selected for an Employment Pass (EP), she will not need an LOC. Once she is granted the EP, she will be granted the work and stay privileges associated with that pass, and she will no longer require the Dependant's Pass. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore.md] In summary: the common-law spouse needs an LOC from their employer, while the legally married spouse's DP becomes superseded by the EP once it is issued—no separate authorization step is needed for the DP itself.Correctly distinguishes LOC for common-law spouse vs EP for legally married spouse, but omits the explicit requirement that the DP must be cancelled before the EP is issued, instead saying it becomes 'superseded' with no separate step needed.
Question:
We have an ONE Pass holder whose common-law spouse currently holds a Long-Term Visit Pass. The spouse has now received a job offer and wants to work here. Also, the ONE Pass holder's legally married spouse is on a Dependant's Pass and has also been offered a role — but she's actually been selected for an EP. Do both spouses need the same type of authorization to start working, and what do we need to do with the DP before the EP is issued?
Expected:
No, the two spouses need different types of authorization:

Common-law spouse on LTVP: Needs a Letter of Consent to work. The prospective employer applies on their behalf, processing takes around 8 weeks, and the LTVP must have at least 3 months validity before applying.

Legally married spouse on Dependant's Pass: Since she's been selected for an EP, she'll work under that Employment Pass — not via a Letter of Consent. The EP gives her independent work and stay privileges.

What you must do with the DP: You need to cancel the Dependant's Pass before the EP is issued. Once she holds an EP, her stay in Singapore no longer depends on the ONE Pass holder, so the DP is no longer required and must be cancelled first.
Actual:
No, the two spouses do not need the same type of authorization, and there are different steps required for each.

**For the common-law spouse (on Long-Term Visit Pass):**
They need a Letter of Consent (LOC) to work in Singapore. The prospective employer must download and submit the LOC (Common-Law Spouse) application form with supporting documents on their behalf. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/index.md]

**For the legally married spouse (on Dependant's Pass):**
Since she has been selected for an Employment Pass (EP), she will not need an LOC. Once she is granted the EP, she will be granted the work and stay privileges associated with that pass, and she will no longer require the Dependant's Pass. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/dependants-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore.md]

In summary: the common-law spouse needs an LOC from their employer, while the legally married spouse's DP becomes superseded by the EP once it is issued—no separate authorization step is needed for the DP itself.

Correctly distinguishes LOC for common-law spouse vs EP for legally married spouse, but omits the explicit requirement that the DP must be cancelled before the EP is issued, instead saying it becomes 'superseded' with no separate step needed.

q-0056hard-12I'm renewing my Employment Pass and I want to understand what gets verified during the process. I know MOM checks my income somehow, and I also recall having to deal with my university qualifications when I first applied. Can you explain how my income is verified with IRAS during renewal, and what I should do if my university's name doesn't appear in the dropdown when I need to submit educational verification proof?# Income Verification and Educational Qualification Verification During EP Renewal ## Income Verification with IRAS During renewal, the Comptroller of Income Tax verifies your income based on your assessment record for the current Year of Assessment. If that record isn't available or finalized yet, the verification will use your assessment records from the two previous Years of Assessment instead. The results are then communicated to the Controller of Work Passes. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/ep_sponsor_renewal_form.md] ## Educational Qualification Verification If your educational institution is not listed in the application form dropdown, you will need to provide verification proof that your declared qualification is authentic **and** that the institution is accredited (recognized by the local government). You can obtain this verification proof from background screening companies listed on MOM's website. When filling in the awarding institution field, select the institution based on exactly what is stated on your certificate—be careful of institution names that may look similar, as a missing word can make a difference. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-booklet.md] **Good news:** Once your qualifications have been verified during your initial application, there is no need to get them verified again at renewal or when changing employers. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md]Income verification is accurate. For educational qualification, the chatbot mentions needing verification proof and being careful with institution names, but misses the key reference guidance: enter the institution's most recent name, or choose not to declare the qualification if unable to find it, and enter qualification level/faculty as per documents.
Question:
I'm renewing my Employment Pass and I want to understand what gets verified during the process. I know MOM checks my income somehow, and I also recall having to deal with my university qualifications when I first applied. Can you explain how my income is verified with IRAS during renewal, and what I should do if my university's name doesn't appear in the dropdown when I need to submit educational verification proof?
Expected:
For income verification during EP renewal, the applicant consents to the Comptroller of Income Tax verifying the income stated in the current and renewal applications based on the assessment record for the current Year of Assessment. If that assessment record is not available or finalised at the point of verification, the Comptroller of Income Tax will verify income against the assessment records for the two previous Years of Assessment. The results of the verification are then communicated by the Comptroller of Income Tax to the Controller of Work Passes.

Regarding educational qualification verification: if the institution name on the educational certificate or verification proof is not available in the dropdown menu, the applicant should enter the institution's most recent name where applicable. Alternatively, the applicant may choose not to declare the qualification if they are unable to find it on the list. The applicant should also enter the qualification level as per the verification proof or educational certificate, and enter the faculty as per the transcript if it is not found in the verification proof or educational certificate.
Actual:
# Income Verification and Educational Qualification Verification During EP Renewal

## Income Verification with IRAS

During renewal, the Comptroller of Income Tax verifies your income based on your assessment record for the current Year of Assessment. If that record isn't available or finalized yet, the verification will use your assessment records from the two previous Years of Assessment instead. The results are then communicated to the Controller of Work Passes. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/ep_sponsor_renewal_form.md]

## Educational Qualification Verification

If your educational institution is not listed in the application form dropdown, you will need to provide verification proof that your declared qualification is authentic **and** that the institution is accredited (recognized by the local government). You can obtain this verification proof from background screening companies listed on MOM's website.

When filling in the awarding institution field, select the institution based on exactly what is stated on your certificate—be careful of institution names that may look similar, as a missing word can make a difference. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-booklet.md]

**Good news:** Once your qualifications have been verified during your initial application, there is no need to get them verified again at renewal or when changing employers. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/renew-a-pass/index.md]

For educational qualification, the chatbot mentions needing verification proof and being careful with institution names, but misses the key reference guidance: enter the institution's most recent name, or choose not to declare the qualification if unable to find it, and enter qualification level/faculty as per documents.

q-0057hard-11We have an ONE Pass holder whose passport is expiring soon, and he also wants to bring his 22-year-old unmarried son to Singapore. Can I update the passport details via a written request to MOM, and can I apply for a Dependant's Pass for his son at the same time?I don't have enough information to answer that from the knowledge base. Regarding your second question: your ONE Pass holder cannot apply for a Dependant's Pass for his 22-year-old unmarried son. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/index.md] Dependant's Passes are only available for unmarried children under 21 years old. Question:
We have an ONE Pass holder whose passport is expiring soon, and he also wants to bring his 22-year-old unmarried son to Singapore. Can I update the passport details via a written request to MOM, and can I apply for a Dependant's Pass for his son at the same time?
Expected:
For the passport update: You can update the passport number and expiry date instantly using the EP eService. Written requests to update these two details will no longer be accepted, so a written request will not work.

For the 22-year-old son: Passes for family members are not available for children who are married or over the age of 21. Since the son is 22, he is not eligible for a family pass under the ONE Pass, even though he is unmarried. He may choose to obtain his own work pass if he wishes to work in Singapore.

Additionally, note that although you can apply for family passes at the same time as the Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass, you may lose the application fees for each family pass if the ONE Pass application gets rejected.

Question:
We're hiring a digital forensics analyst who is 28 years old. We want to understand what salary benchmarks apply under COMPASS C1 for someone at that age, and also what qualifications or experience this role requires to qualify for the C5 skills bonus on the Shortage Occupation List.
Expected:
For a 28-year-old candidate, the COMPASS C1 salary benchmarks are: $8,600 to earn 10 points (65th percentile of local PMETs in ICT field) and $12,994 to earn 20 points (90th percentile of local PMETs in ICT field). For the C5 skills bonus under the Shortage Occupation List, the role of Digital forensics analyst is eligible, and the candidate must meet either of two requirements: (1a) at least a Bachelor's degree in one of the following faculties — Computer Science, Information Technology, Programming & Systems Analysis, or Science (Computer Studies); OR (1b) a minimum total of three years' work experience in at least one of these job titles: Computer forensics expert, Computer forensics investigator, Cyber forensics expert, Cyber forensics investigation specialist, Cyber incident investigation specialist, Cyber threat investigation specialist, Digital forensic expert, Digital forensics analyst, ICT forensics expert, or Information forensics expert. The supporting agency for this SOL occupation is the Infocomm Media Development Authority (IMDA). The job duties include conducting complex post-incident forensic analysis to investigate causes of intrusion, attack, loss, or breach; identifying and defining forensic issues and root causes; developing reports detailing incident timeline, evidence, findings, conclusions and recommendations; supporting the design of a breach notification process after a security incident; selecting, testing, and deploying security products and collecting information about Internet-based criminal activities; and recommending corrective actions to prevent and mitigate internal control failures.
Actual:
I can answer part of your question, but I don't have complete information on both points.

**On C1 Salary Benchmarks:**

The C1 criterion requires your candidate's fixed monthly salary to meet or exceed the 65th percentile of local PMET salaries in your sector [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/index.md]. The specific benchmark amount depends on your organisation's sector classification. The benchmarks are updated annually and can be found in the C1 salary benchmarks document by sector. You can also check your organisation's sector classification online [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/index.md].

**On C5 Skills Bonus Requirements:**

I don't have enough information to answer what specific qualifications or experience a digital forensics analyst needs for the C5 skills bonus. The CONTEXT indicates that to obtain C5 bonus points, your candidate must perform the job duties listed for the specific shortage occupation and meet additional requirements stated in the SOL Employer Guide [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md]. However, the specific requirements for the digital forensics analyst role are not included in the available excerpts. You should consult the SOL Employer Guide for those details.

q-0059hard-11We have an ONE Pass holder who wants to bring over his 22-year-old unmarried son who has a disability, and also wants to start working in a restricted occupation. What type of pass can the son get, and what does the ONE Pass holder need to do before he can begin work in that restricted occupation?Based on the knowledge base, I can answer part of your question: **For the son's pass:** Your 22-year-old unmarried son with a disability can obtain a [Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP)](source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/index.md). The LTVP is available for handicapped children aged 21 and above who are unmarried [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/index.md]. **For the ONE Pass holder's restricted occupation work:** I don't have enough information in the knowledge base to answer what specific steps or approvals are needed before working in a restricted occupation. The CONTEXT mentions there may be "additional conditions that I need to meet for certain occupations," but the details are not included in the excerpts provided. You would need to consult MOM directly or access the full guidance on occupational restrictions for ONE Pass holders.Correctly identifies LTVP for the son, but fails to provide the required information about restricted occupation procedures (submitting request via go.gov.sg/request-restricted-occupation, waiting for approval, 3-week response time), which is a key part of the question.
Question:
We have an ONE Pass holder who wants to bring over his 22-year-old unmarried son who has a disability, and also wants to start working in a restricted occupation. What type of pass can the son get, and what does the ONE Pass holder need to do before he can begin work in that restricted occupation?
Expected:
The 22-year-old unmarried handicapped child of an Overseas Networks & Expertise Pass (ONE Pass) holder is eligible for a Long-Term Visit Pass (not a Dependant's Pass, which is only for unmarried children under 21). To work in a restricted occupation, the ONE Pass holder must submit a request to MOM using their online form (at go.gov.sg/request-restricted-occupation) before starting work in that occupation. The holder can only begin work in the restricted occupation after the request has been approved, and MOM will respond within 3 weeks in most cases. A full list of restricted occupations is available from MOM.
Actual:
Based on the knowledge base, I can answer part of your question:

**For the son's pass:** Your 22-year-old unmarried son with a disability can obtain a [Long-Term Visit Pass (LTVP)](source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/index.md). The LTVP is available for handicapped children aged 21 and above who are unmarried [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/index.md].

**For the ONE Pass holder's restricted occupation work:** I don't have enough information in the knowledge base to answer what specific steps or approvals are needed before working in a restricted occupation. The CONTEXT mentions there may be "additional conditions that I need to meet for certain occupations," but the details are not included in the excerpts provided. You would need to consult MOM directly or access the full guidance on occupational restrictions for ONE Pass holders.
Judge remark (scored 1):
Correctly identifies LTVP for the son, but fails to provide the required information about restricted occupation procedures (submitting request via go.gov.sg/request-restricted-occupation, waiting for approval, 3-week response time), which is a key part of the question.
