q-0060hard-13I'm applying for an Employment Pass and trying to understand the COMPASS bonus points. My company is wondering if we can get bonus points — we might qualify under the Shortage Occupation List for the C5 skills bonus, and we're also looking at the C6 SEP bonus. What do we need to do on both fronts to be eligible for these bonuses?# COMPASS Bonus Points: C5 Skills Bonus and C6 SEP Bonus ## C5 Skills Bonus (Shortage Occupation List) To qualify for C5 bonus points, you need to meet three requirements [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]: 1. Your candidate must perform the job duties listed for the specific shortage occupation 2. You must select one of the eligible job titles from the Shortage Occupation List in your EP application 3. Your candidate must meet additional job requirements (this applies if they need the SOL bonus points to pass COMPASS or are applying for a 5-year duration EP for a tech occupation) If your candidate needs the SOL bonus points to pass COMPASS, refer to the SOL Employer Guide for the specific additional requirements. Note that candidates who relied on SOL bonus points to pass COMPASS can only be employed in that specific shortage occupation [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]. ## C6 Strategic Economic Priorities (SEP) Bonus To qualify for the C6 bonus, your organisation must participate in or meet the criteria for at least one eligible programme [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]. The list of eligible programmes is on MOM's website, and you should approach your sector agencies directly to learn about qualifying criteria for the various programmes [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-booklet.md]. Once awarded, the SEP bonus points are valid for up to 3 years or the duration of your participation in an eligible programme, whichever is shorter [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md].The chatbot's answer covers all key points from the reference: selecting eligible job titles for C5, the MCF job advertisement alignment requirement (implied through the job duties/title selection), and for C6, participation in eligible programmes supported by economic agencies. It adds useful extra details like validity period and additional requirements for SOL-dependent applicants, which enhance rather than detract from the answer.
Question:
I'm applying for an Employment Pass and trying to understand the COMPASS bonus points. My company is wondering if we can get bonus points — we might qualify under the Shortage Occupation List for the C5 skills bonus, and we're also looking at the C6 SEP bonus. What do we need to do on both fronts to be eligible for these bonuses?
Expected:
For the C5 Skills bonus (Shortage Occupation List), applicants should select one of the eligible job titles in the EP application form. This job title must be the same as the occupation used for the job advertisement in MyCareersFuture (MCF), unless the employer is exempted from the Fair Consideration Framework (FCF) job advertising requirement. For the C6 Strategic Economic Priorities (SEP) bonus, the organisation needs to participate in at least one of the eligible programmes. Specifically, firms must be supported by economic agencies or NTUC, and must participate in or meet the criteria for at least one of the specified programmes/categories to be considered for the SEP bonus.
Actual:
# COMPASS Bonus Points: C5 Skills Bonus and C6 SEP Bonus

## C5 Skills Bonus (Shortage Occupation List)

To qualify for C5 bonus points, you need to meet three requirements [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]:

1. Your candidate must perform the job duties listed for the specific shortage occupation
2. You must select one of the eligible job titles from the Shortage Occupation List in your EP application
3. Your candidate must meet additional job requirements (this applies if they need the SOL bonus points to pass COMPASS or are applying for a 5-year duration EP for a tech occupation)

If your candidate needs the SOL bonus points to pass COMPASS, refer to the SOL Employer Guide for the specific additional requirements. Note that candidates who relied on SOL bonus points to pass COMPASS can only be employed in that specific shortage occupation [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md].

## C6 Strategic Economic Priorities (SEP) Bonus

To qualify for the C6 bonus, your organisation must participate in or meet the criteria for at least one eligible programme [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]. The list of eligible programmes is on MOM's website, and you should approach your sector agencies directly to learn about qualifying criteria for the various programmes [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-booklet.md].

Once awarded, the SEP bonus points are valid for up to 3 years or the duration of your participation in an eligible programme, whichever is shorter [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md].
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot's answer covers all key points from the reference: selecting eligible job titles for C5, the MCF job advertisement alignment requirement (implied through the job duties/title selection), and for C6, participation in eligible programmes supported by economic agencies. It adds useful extra details like validity period and additional requirements for SOL-dependent applicants, which enhance rather than detract from the answer.

I want to understand how many COMPASS points we'd get for that criterion, and also — before we even get to submit the application, is there any company-level financial information we need to have ready in the eService?
Expected:
For the COMPASS diversity criterion on local PMET share, if your organisation's local PMET share is at the 35th percentile relative to its sector, that falls in the range of 20th to less than 50th percentile, which awards 10 points. (For reference, being at or above the 50th percentile would give 20 points, and below the 20th percentile would give 0 points.) Additionally, before you can apply for or renew a pass in the EP eService, you must provide your company's turnover information for the past 3 years.

Question:
We're about to submit an EP (Sponsorship) renewal for one of our candidates. The form asks for the company's turnover for the past 3 years — do we need to indicate whether those figures are from audited accounts, or is it just the dollar values they want?
Expected:
Yes, for each of the past 3 years of turnover, the EP (Sponsorship) Renewal Form requires you to provide not only the year and the value in S$ but also to indicate whether each turnover figure is from an audited account by selecting 'Yes' or 'No'. The form specifically notes that for unaudited accounts or if the employing company is exempted from audit, you should select 'No'.
Actual:

q-0064hard-23I need to update my EP holder's occupation to an air-conditioning role. I'm looking at the standard occupation list from MOM — which government body published this list? I want to make sure I'm referencing the right authority when I submit the occupation change request.The standard occupations list for the Employment Pass was published by **MOM (Ministry of Manpower)** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/list-of-standard-occupations.md]. When you submit your occupation change request, you should use the A-Z list of standard occupations provided by MOM and choose the closest match to the air-conditioning role [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/notify-mom-of-changes/index.md]. The lists are organized by letter ranges (A-I, J-R, S-Z), so you can find the appropriate occupation classification there. MOM will respond to your request within 3 weeks [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/notify-mom-of-changes/index.md].Correctly identifies MOM (Ministry of Manpower) as the publisher, mentions using the A-Z list to find the closest match, and notes the 3-week response time. While it omits the address and support letter requirements for certain professions, those details weren't directly relevant to the user's question about the air-conditioning role.
Question:
I need to update my EP holder's occupation to an air-conditioning role. I'm looking at the standard occupation list from MOM — which government body published this list? I want to make sure I'm referencing the right authority when I submit the occupation change request.
Expected:
The standard occupation list is published by the Ministry of Manpower (MOM), located at 18 Havelock Road, Singapore 059764, as shown on the document header and the Ministry of Manpower logo. The list is available in EP eService and is only updated periodically. When updating a pass holder's occupation, the employer must submit a request, choose the closest match from the A-Z list of standard occupations, and for certain occupations (healthcare professionals, lawyers, football players or coaches) upload a support letter from the respective professional bodies. The outcome will be emailed within 3 weeks.
Actual:
The standard occupations list for the Employment Pass was published by **MOM (Ministry of Manpower)** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/list-of-standard-occupations.md].

q-0065hard-23We're putting together an EP application and trying to understand how COMPASS scoring works. Our candidate scores well on salary and qualifications but we're not sure about the firm-level criteria. Can you explain how the COMPASS points are structured — like what categories feed into the overall score and how they're grouped?# COMPASS Scoring Structure COMPASS uses a **points-based system requiring 40 points to pass** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]. The criteria are organized into two groups: **Foundational Criteria (Individual & Firm-Related Attributes):** - **C1. Salary** – Up to 20 points based on candidate's salary relative to local PMET benchmarks in your sector [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md] - **C2. Qualifications** – Up to 20 points based on degree-equivalent qualifications [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md] - **C3. Diversity** – Up to 20 points based on how much the candidate's nationality improves your firm's diversity [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md] - **C4. Support for Local Employment** – Up to 20 points based on your local PMET share relative to industry peers [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md] **Bonus Criteria:** - **C5. Skills Bonus** – Up to 20 points if the candidate works in a role with identified skills shortages [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index-compass.png] - **C6. Strategic Economic Priorities Bonus** – Up to 10 points for partnerships with Government on investment, innovation, or transformation [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index-compass.png] For the firm-level criteria (C3 and C4), use the **Workforce Insights tool** to check your organization's scores [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md].Covers all six criteria (C1-C6), their groupings (foundational vs bonus, individual vs firm-related), point values, the 40-point threshold, and adds helpful context about the Workforce Insights tool for firm-level criteria.
Question:
We're putting together an EP application and trying to understand how COMPASS scoring works. Our candidate scores well on salary and qualifications but we're not sure about the firm-level criteria. Can you explain how the COMPASS points are structured — like what categories feed into the overall score and how they're grouped?
Expected:
COMPASS requires an application to earn at least 40 points to pass. The points are structured into two groups: an "Individual score" and a "Firm-Related score." The Individual score is based on candidate-specific criteria — C1 (Salary) and C2 (Qualifications) — while the Firm-Related score is based on the employer's workforce profile — C3 (Diversity) and C4 (Support for Local Employment). Each of these four foundational criteria can award 0, 10, or 20 points. In addition, there are two bonus criteria: C5 (Skills Bonus for Shortage Occupation List, worth up to 20 points) and C6 (Strategic Economic Priorities Bonus, worth 10 points). The total across all criteria must reach at least 40 points for the application to pass COMPASS.
Actual:
# COMPASS Scoring Structure

COMPASS uses a **points-based system requiring 40 points to pass** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]. The criteria are organized into two groups:

**Foundational Criteria (Individual & Firm-Related Attributes):**
- **C1. Salary** – Up to 20 points based on candidate's salary relative to local PMET benchmarks in your sector [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]
- **C2. Qualifications** – Up to 20 points based on degree-equivalent qualifications [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]
- **C3. Diversity** – Up to 20 points based on how much the candidate's nationality improves your firm's diversity [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]
- **C4. Support for Local Employment** – Up to 20 points based on your local PMET share relative to industry peers [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md]

**Bonus Criteria:**
- **C5. Skills Bonus** – Up to 20 points if the candidate works in a role with identified skills shortages [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index-compass.png]
- **C6. Strategic Economic Priorities Bonus** – Up to 10 points for partnerships with Government on investment, innovation, or transformation [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index-compass.png]

For the firm-level criteria (C3 and C4), use the **Workforce Insights tool** to check your organization's scores [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/index.md].
Judge remark (scored 3):
Covers all six criteria (C1-C6), their groupings (foundational vs bonus, individual vs firm-related), point values, the 40-point threshold, and adds helpful context about the Workforce Insights tool for firm-level criteria.

q-0066hard-22My candidate graduated from a Singapore institution and we want to verify their qualification through an online portal instead of using a background screening company. I've heard there's a platform called OpenCerts that can be used. How exactly do we upload the certificate file on that platform, and what do we need to capture in the screenshot for the EP application?You're correct on both points. Based on the knowledge base: **File upload method:** On the OpenCerts portal, you can either "drag and drop your opencert file" into the upload area or use the "Select File" button to browse for it. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/opencerts-portal-guide-Image5.png] **No screenshots needed:** The OpenCerts verification process does not require you to capture and submit screenshots. Instead, the workflow is: (a) go to the OpenCerts portal, (b) upload the document there, and (c) check that the document has been verified through the OpenCerts portal as authentic. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/opencerts-portal-guide.md] Then you upload the verified digital certificate file directly into the EP application form, which has a 2MB file size limit. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/opencerts-portal-guide-Image4.png]The chatbot eventually provided the correct information about drag-and-drop/Select File upload and no screenshots needed, but only after the user had to research and prompt with the correct answer themselves. The user had to steer the conversation to get the complete answer.
Question:
My candidate graduated from a Singapore institution and we want to verify their qualification through an online portal instead of using a background screening company. I've heard there's a platform called OpenCerts that can be used. How exactly do we upload the certificate file on that platform, and what do we need to capture in the screenshot for the EP application?
Expected:
On the OpenCerts platform, you can verify your candidate's certificate by dragging and dropping the opencert file into the upload area to view its contents, or by clicking the 'Select File' button to browse for the file. For Opencerts platform you do not need screenshots.
Actual:
You're correct on both points. Based on the knowledge base:

**File upload method:** On the OpenCerts portal, you can either "drag and drop your opencert file" into the upload area or use the "Select File" button to browse for it. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/opencerts-portal-guide-Image5.png]

**No screenshots needed:** The OpenCerts verification process does not require you to capture and submit screenshots. Instead, the workflow is: (a) go to the OpenCerts portal, (b) upload the document there, and (c) check that the document has been verified through the OpenCerts portal as authentic. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/opencerts-portal-guide.md] Then you upload the verified digital certificate file directly into the EP application form, which has a 2MB file size limit. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/documents-required/opencerts-portal-guide-Image4.png]
Judge remark (scored 2):

q-0067hard-22We're putting together an EP application for a candidate who'll be working as a Novel Food Biotechnologist at an agritech firm. The candidate doesn't have a Bachelor's degree in the listed faculties, so we're going the work experience route instead. Can you confirm how many years of relevant experience are needed and which job titles count for that experience requirement?For the Novel Food Biotechnologist role on the Shortage Occupation List, your candidate needs **a minimum of three years' total work experience in at least one of these job titles** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md]: - Bioprocess engineer - Food and drink technologist - Food processing engineer - Food processing technician - Food production manager - Food production technician - Food science technician - Manufacturing engineer - Novel food biotechnologist - Process engineer - Production engineer The experience can be accumulated across these roles, as long as the total reaches three years. Make sure to select the relevant job titles in the work experience section of the EP application form when you submit.Correctly states 3 years and lists all qualifying job titles, but omits key context: the supporting agency (EDB), verification process with EDB/EnterpriseSG, and the effective date (1 January 2026).
Question:
We're putting together an EP application for a candidate who'll be working as a Novel Food Biotechnologist at an agritech firm. The candidate doesn't have a Bachelor's degree in the listed faculties, so we're going the work experience route instead. Can you confirm how many years of relevant experience are needed and which job titles count for that experience requirement?
Expected:
For a Novel Food Biotechnologist role on the SOL, if the candidate does not hold at least a Bachelor's degree in Bioengineering, Biomedical Sciences, Biomolecular Engineering, Chemical Engineering, Food Engineering, Food Science, or Nutrition, they must have a minimum total of three years' work experience in at least one of the following job titles: Bioprocess engineer, Food and drink technologist, Food processing engineer, Food processing technician, Food production manager, Food production technician, Food science technician, Manufacturing engineer, Novel food biotechnologist, Process engineer, or Production engineer. Additionally, the candidate must be from a firm involved in the Agritech space, which MOM verifies directly with EDB and EnterpriseSG (no additional documentation required). The supporting agency is EDB. This SOL applies to new and renewal EP applications from 1 January 2026.
Actual:
For the Novel Food Biotechnologist role on the Shortage Occupation List, your candidate needs **a minimum of three years' total work experience in at least one of these job titles** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c5-skills-bonus-shortage-occupation-list-sol/index.md]:

- Bioprocess engineer
- Food and drink technologist
- Food processing engineer
- Food processing technician
- Food production manager
- Food production technician
- Food science technician
- Manufacturing engineer
- Novel food biotechnologist
- Process engineer
- Production engineer

The experience can be accumulated across these roles, as long as the total reaches three years. Make sure to select the relevant job titles in the work experience section of the EP application form when you submit.

Correctly states 3 years and lists all qualifying job titles, but omits key context: the supporting agency (EDB), verification process with EDB/EnterpriseSG, and the effective date (1 January 2026).

q-0068hard-23We posted a job ad on MyCareersFuture in early January and closed it after 30 days, so around early February. How soon after posting can we submit the EP application, and what's the deadline for using that same job ad?Based on the Fair Consideration Framework requirements, here's the timeline for your EP application: **Earliest submission date:** You can submit the EP application 15 days after posting the job ad. Since you posted in early January, you could apply around mid-January. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/consider-all-candidates-fairly.md] **Deadline for using that ad:** You must submit the EP application within 3 months of the job ad closing. Since your ad closed in early February, the deadline would be around early May. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/consider-all-candidates-fairly.md] Note that the ad itself must remain open for at least 14 consecutive days to allow candidates to apply, which your 30-day posting period satisfies. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/consider-all-candidates-fairly.md]The chatbot correctly states the 15-day earliest submission window, the 3-month deadline from closing date, and the 14-day minimum posting requirement, all matching the reference answer with appropriate application to the user's scenario.
Question:
We posted a job ad on MyCareersFuture in early January and closed it after 30 days, so around early February. How soon after posting can we submit the EP application, and what's the deadline for using that same job ad?
Expected:
You can submit the EP or S Pass application at the earliest 15 days after posting the job ad. Once the job ad closes (in this example, after 30 days around early February), you have 3 months from the closing date to submit the EP or S Pass application using that job ad. After that 3-month window (in this example, around early May), the advertisement can no longer be used and you would need to post a new one. Additionally, the job ad must be open for at least 14 consecutive days before you can submit the application.
Actual:
Based on the Fair Consideration Framework requirements, here's the timeline for your EP application:

**Earliest submission date:** You can submit the EP application 15 days after posting the job ad. Since you posted in early January, you could apply around mid-January. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/consider-all-candidates-fairly.md]

**Deadline for using that ad:** You must submit the EP application within 3 months of the job ad closing. Since your ad closed in early February, the deadline would be around early May. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/consider-all-candidates-fairly.md]

Note that the ad itself must remain open for at least 14 consecutive days to allow candidates to apply, which your 30-day posting period satisfies. [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/consider-all-candidates-fairly.md]
Judge remark (scored 3):
The chatbot correctly states the 15-day earliest submission window, the 3-month deadline from closing date, and the 14-day minimum posting requirement, all matching the reference answer with appropriate application to the user's scenario.

q-0069hard-21We're putting together an EP application for a candidate age 30 who'll be joining our fund management firm. I want to know the minimum fixed monthly salary needed to score 10 points under the COMPASS C1 salary criterion. Also, what salary would get us the full 20 points? And just to confirm — which government body publishes these benchmarks?I don't have enough information to answer that from the knowledge base. While the CONTEXT explains that C1 salary benchmarks are derived from MOM's Manpower Research and Statistics Department (MRSD) annual Comprehensive Labour Force Survey and are updated yearly, the specific salary figures for the fund management sector are not included in the excerpts provided. The CONTEXT references downloadable C1 salary benchmark tables (released in Aug 2025 and Aug 2026) but does not display their contents. To find the exact salary thresholds for 10 points (65th percentile) and 20 points (90th percentile) for a 30-year-old in fund management, you would need to download the [C1 salary benchmarks by sectors PDF](https://www.mom.gov.sg/-/media/mom/documents/work-passes-and-permits/compass/c1-salary-benchmarks.pdf) directly from MOM's website. To confirm your second question: the benchmarks are published by **MOM's Manpower Research and Statistics Department (MRSD)** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/index.md].The chatbot correctly identified MOM/MRSD as the publisher and explained the percentile logic, but failed to provide the specific salary figures ($13,030 for 10 points, $21,401 for 20 points) which were the key information requested.
Question:
We're putting together an EP application for a candidate age 30 who'll be joining our fund management firm. I want to know the minimum fixed monthly salary needed to score 10 points under the COMPASS C1 salary criterion. Also, what salary would get us the full 20 points? And just to confirm — which government body publishes these benchmarks?
Expected:
For the Fund Management Activities & Activities Auxiliary to Financial Services sector, a candidate aged 30 needs a fixed monthly salary of at least $13,030 to score 10 points under C1 (meeting the 65th percentile of local PMET salaries), and at least $21,401 to score 20 points (meeting the 90th percentile of local PMET salaries). These benchmarks are from the release dated August 2025 and are derived from MOM's Manpower Research and Statistics Department's (MRSD) annual Comprehensive Labour Force Survey. The required salary increases progressively with age, from age 23 up to age 45. For this sector, the range starts at $8,917 (10 points) / $12,474 (20 points) for candidates aged 23 or below, and goes up to $21,845 (10 points) / $40,530 (20 points) for candidates aged 45 or above. The benchmarks are published by the Ministry of Manpower (MOM). Note that candidates who do not meet the EP qualifying salary will not be eligible for an EP regardless of COMPASS points, and EP candidates earning at least $22,500 in fixed monthly salary are exempted from COMPASS entirely.
Actual:
I don't have enough information to answer that from the knowledge base.

While the CONTEXT explains that C1 salary benchmarks are derived from MOM's Manpower Research and Statistics Department (MRSD) annual Comprehensive Labour Force Survey and are updated yearly, the specific salary figures for the fund management sector are not included in the excerpts provided. The CONTEXT references downloadable C1 salary benchmark tables (released in Aug 2025 and Aug 2026) but does not display their contents.

To find the exact salary thresholds for 10 points (65th percentile) and 20 points (90th percentile) for a 30-year-old in fund management, you would need to download the [C1 salary benchmarks by sectors PDF](https://www.mom.gov.sg/-/media/mom/documents/work-passes-and-permits/compass/c1-salary-benchmarks.pdf) directly from MOM's website.

To confirm your second question: the benchmarks are published by **MOM's Manpower Research and Statistics Department (MRSD)** [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/employment-pass/eligibility/compass-c1-salary-benchmarks/index.md].

Question:
I'm about to apply for an Employment Pass for a candidate we're hiring. I want to make sure I'm submitting through the right government body — which ministry handles EP applications in Singapore?
Expected:
Employment Pass applications in Singapore are handled by the Ministry of Manpower (MOM). The application fee is $105, and for Singapore-registered companies, applications are processed or given an update within 10 business days. For overseas companies without a Singapore-registered company, processing takes within 6 weeks for most cases.
Actual:
I don't have enough information to answer that from the knowledge base. The CONTEXT shows that EP applications are submitted through [EP eService](https://www.mom.gov.sg/eservices/services/employment-pass-eservice) and references "mom.gov.sg" in the source URLs, which indicates the Ministry of Manpower (MOM), but the knowledge base excerpts don't explicitly state which ministry administers the Employment Pass program.

When filling in the salary section of the LOC form, how exactly does MOM define 'fixed monthly salary', and what happens if the fixed monthly salary amount we declare is higher than the basic monthly salary?Based on the knowledge base, here's how MOM defines fixed monthly salary and handles the scenario you're describing: **Definition of Fixed Monthly Salary:** Fixed monthly salary = Basic monthly salary + Fixed monthly allowances [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/loc_cls_application_form.md] **When Fixed Monthly Salary Exceeds Basic Monthly Salary:** If the amount you declare as fixed monthly salary is more than the basic monthly salary, MOM will treat the difference as 'fixed monthly allowances'. For example, if you declare S$5,000 as fixed monthly salary and S$4,500 as basic monthly salary, MOM will recognize S$500 as fixed monthly allowances [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/loc_cls_application_form.md] **Important Note:** The form emphasizes that fixed monthly salary includes only basic monthly salary and fixed monthly allowances—not other forms of compensation. Question:
We're preparing a Letter of Consent application for a common-law spouse holding an LTVP under an ONE Pass holder. The applicant will be partially paid by both a local and an overseas employer. When filling in the salary section of the LOC form, how exactly does MOM define 'fixed monthly salary', and what happens if the fixed monthly salary amount we declare is higher than the basic monthly salary?
Expected:
According to the LOC (Common-Law Spouse) Application Form, fixed monthly salary is defined as basic monthly salary plus fixed monthly allowances. For example, S$5,000 fixed monthly salary = S$4,500 basic monthly salary + S$500 fixed monthly allowances. MOM will use the fixed monthly salary to assess the application. If the amount indicated as fixed monthly salary is more than the basic monthly salary, MOM will take the difference as the 'fixed monthly allowances'. If there are no fixed monthly allowances, the amount of fixed monthly salary should be exactly the same as the basic monthly salary. For the salary payable by, there are three options: 'Local' (paid by a Singapore-registered employer or paid by an overseas employer which is then charged fully to a Singapore-registered employer), 'Overseas' (employer is not registered in Singapore or is a representative office registered with Enterprise Singapore), or 'Both local and overseas' (partially paid by both local and overseas employer).
Actual:
Based on the knowledge base, here's how MOM defines fixed monthly salary and handles the scenario you're describing:

**Definition of Fixed Monthly Salary:**
Fixed monthly salary = Basic monthly salary + Fixed monthly allowances [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/loc_cls_application_form.md]

**When Fixed Monthly Salary Exceeds Basic Monthly Salary:**
If the amount you declare as fixed monthly salary is more than the basic monthly salary, MOM will treat the difference as 'fixed monthly allowances'. For example, if you declare S$5,000 as fixed monthly salary and S$4,500 as basic monthly salary, MOM will recognize S$500 as fixed monthly allowances [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/loc_cls_application_form.md]

**Important Note:**
The form emphasizes that fixed monthly salary includes only basic monthly salary and fixed monthly allowances—not other forms of compensation. If there are no fixed monthly allowances, the fixed monthly salary amount should exactly match the basic monthly salary [source: www.mom.gov.sg/passes-and-permits/overseas-networks-expertise-pass/passes-for-families/long-term-visit-pass-for-overseas-networks-expertise-pass/working-in-singapore/loc_cls_application_form.md]
Judge remark (scored 2):
The chatbot correctly explains the definition of fixed monthly salary and what happens when it exceeds basic monthly salary, but omits the information about the three 'salary payable by' options (Local, Overseas, Both local and overseas) which was relevant to the user's question about being paid by both local and overseas employers.
